SECTION 501. INTRODUCTION 

Public, No. 2, Seventy-third Congress, March 20, 1933, as amended, and the Veterans Regulations issued thereunder, as amended (38 U. S. C. 701-721, ch. 12, Veterans Regulations), authorize the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs under such limitations as he may prescribe, and within the limits of existing Veterans' Administration facilities, to furnish to persons discharged from the Army, Navy, Marine Corps, or Coast Guard for disabilities incurred in line of duty, or to those in receipt of compensation for service-connected disability, and to veterans of any war, domiciliary care where they are suffering with permanent disabilities, tuberculosis, or neuropsychiatric ailments, and medical and hospital treatment for diseases or injuries. A veteran of any war who is in need of hospitalization or domiciliary care and is unable to defray the necessary expenses therefor may be furnished necessary hospitalization or domiciliary care (including transportation) in any Veterans' Administration hospital or home within the limitations existing in such facilities, irrespective of whether the disability is due to service. A discharge or release from active service under conditions other than dishonorable is a prerequisite to entitlement to hospitalization or domiciliary care.

The term "Veterans' Administration facilities," as used above, means those facilities over which the Veterans' Administration has direct and exclusive jurisdiction, those other Government facilities under contract with the Veterans' Administration, and those private institutions under contract with the Veterans' Administration. Since October 31, 1945, the term "facility" has been discontinued, the terms "hospital," "home," or "center" being substituted therefor. (See sec. 105.) 52

The term "center" is also applied to certain hospitals which have been designated to provide specialized clinical study and treatment in certain types of cases. These include the allergy, amputation, aphasic treatment, chest surgery, diagnostic, laboratory, neurosurgical, paraplegia, plastic surgery, and tumor centers. 

SECTION 502. PREFERENCE IN ADMISSION 

Domiciliary or hospital care, including medical treatment, may be furnished the following persons in the specified order of preference: (1) Hospital treatment for (a) veterans of any war, as defined in 

Veterans Regulation No. 10, as amended (38 U. S. C., ch. 12, Veterans 

Regulations), including those who had active duty as members of the Women's Army Auxiliary Corps, discharged or released from active service under conditions other than dishonorable, when suffering from injuries or diseases incurred or aggravated in line of duty in that period of active military or naval service, and for which they are medically determined to be in need of hospital treatment; (b) retired officers and retired enlisted men, including members of the Fleet Naval Reserve or Marine Corps Reserve on retainer pay, who had honorable service in a period of war and are medically determined to need hospital treatment for an injury or disease incurred in line of duty in active military or naval service (38 U. S. C. 706b), and (c) persons who on or after April 6, 1917, and prior to November 12, 1918, or who on or after December 7, 1941, and prior to 12 noon, December 31, 1946, applied for enlistment or enrollment in the active military or naval forces and who were provisionally accepted and directed or ordered to report to a place for final acceptance into such military or naval service, or who were accepted for service and after reporting pursuant to the call of the local draft board and prior to rejection, or who after being called into the Federal service as members of the National Guard, but before being enrolled for the Federal service, suffered injury or disease in line of duty for which they are receiving disability compensation and for which they are in need of hospital treatment. (2) Hospital treatment for (a) persons who were discharged or released under conditions other than dishonorable from active military or naval service for disability incurred or aggravated in line of duty or who are in receipt of compensation for service-connected or service-aggravated disability, when suffering from injuries or diseases incurred or aggravated in line of duty in such active service and for which they are medically determined to be in need of hospital treatment; (b) persons retired from the Army of the United States under 

Public, No. 18, Seventy-sixth Congress, as amended, and Public Law 

262, Seventy-seventh Congress (10 U. S. C. 456, 456a), who had service only in a period other than wartime and who are suffering from disease or injury incurred or aggravated in line of duty which is medically determined to require hospital treatment; (c) persons retired from the Army, Navy, Marine Corps or Coast Guard, Regular Establishment, not having had war service, who elect to receive compensation under laws administered by the Veterans' Administration in lieu of retirement pay, who are in need of hospital treatment for that disease or injury for which they are receiving disability compensation; and (d) persons who on or after August 27, 1940, and prior to 

December 7, 1941, applied for enlistment or enrollment in the active

military or naval forces and who were provisionally accepted and directed or ordered to report to a place for final acceptance into such military or naval service, or who were selected for service and after reporting pursuant to the call of the local draft board and prior to rejection, or who after being called in the Federal service as members of the National Guard but before being enrolled for the Federal service suffered injury or disease in line of duty for which they are receiving disability compensation and for which they are in need of hospital treatment. (3) (a) Hospital care for persons who were discharged or released from active military or naval service under conditions other than dishonorable for disability incurred or aggravated in line of duty, or who are in receipt of compensation for service-connected or serviceaggravated disability, when suffering from non-service-connected diseases or injuries requiring hospitalization; (b) domiciliary care for persons listed in 3 (a) above when suffering from a permanent disability or tuberculous or neuropsychiatric ailment and who are incapacitated from earning a living and who have no adequate means of support; and (c) hospital treatment for persons retired from the Army of the United States under Public No. 18, Seventy-sixth Congress, as amended, and Public Law 262, Seventy-seventh Congress (10 U. S. 

C. 456, 456a), who had service only in a period other than wartime, in a hospital or center under the direct and exclusive jurisdiction of the Veterans' Administration if beds are available, and they agree to pay the per diem rate to cover subsistence, which is set by the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs. (4) Hospital treatment or domiciliary care for (a) persons who served in the active military or naval forces, including those who had active duty in the Women's Army Auxiliary Corps, regardless of length of service, during a period of war, who were discharged or released from active duty under conditions other than dishonorable, who swear that they are unable to defray the expenses of hospitalization or domiciliary care, and who are suffering from a disability, disease, or defect which, being susceptible of cure or decided improvement, indicates need for hospital care, or which, being essentially chronic in type and not susceptible of cure or decided improvement by hospital care, is producing disablement of such degree and of such probable persistency as will incapacitate from earning a living for a prospective period, and thereby indicates need for domiciliary care; and (b) persons retired from the active military or naval service, including members of the Fleet Naval Reserve or Marine Corps Reserve on retainer pay, who had honorable service in a period of war and who meet the other requirements of 4 (a) hereof (38 

SECTION 503. APPLICATION FOR HOSPITAL TREATMENT OR DOMICILIARY CARE Veterans' Administration Form 10-P-10, Application for Hospital 

Treatment or Domiciliary Care, should be executed by the applicant, nearest relative, guardian, or representative and forwarded to the nearest Veterans' Administration field station.

1 

SECTION 504. HOSPITALIZATION IN EMERGENCIES 

Emergency hospitalization may be granted under certain conditions but before proceeding to a Veterans' Administration hospital the veteran or someone acting for him should communicate with the nearest Veterans' Administration field station by telephone or telegraph and request authority for admission and for transportation if necessary.

SECTION 505. TRANSPORTATION 

When prior authorization is obtained from the Veterans' Administration, transportation may be supplied at Government expense to eligible claimants or beneficiaries whose admission to a station is authorized for observation and examination, or for hospital treatment of service-connected conditions, or for hospital or domiciliary care for non-service-connected conditions when a sworn statement is made on their application that they are unable to defray transportation expenses and, except under certain circumstances, for out-patient examination or out-patient treatment for service-connected conditions, including adjunct treatment and for non-service-connected conditions to prevent interruption of a course of vocational training authorized by Public Law 16, Seventy-eighth Congress, March 24, 1943, as amended. Upon completion of hospitalization for treatment or for observation and examination, and regular discharge, or completion of out-patient service, return transportation may be supplied a claimant or beneficiary to the point from which he had proceeded or to some other point if no additional expense will be occasioned thereby. Meals and lodgings en route, pullman accommodations, and accompaniment by an attendant or attendants may be authorized when determined necessary for the travel.

SECTION 506. CLOTHING 

Veterans who are receiving domiciliary or hospital care in Veterans' Administration hospitals or homes may be supplied with clothing at Government expense when both of the following conditions are met: (1) When necessary for the protection of health or for sanitary reasons; and (2) When the beneficiary is without means and is receiving less than $20 per month from any source or although in receipt of $20 or j more monthly, is continuously contributing any portion thereof to dependents so as to reduce his monthly income below $20 for his personal use. However, any or all funds in excess of $75, regardless of the source, which may accumulate from income of less than $20 monthly will be applied to purchase of required clothing. 

Clothing furnished beneficiaries, or personally owned by them, may be altered, repaired, cleaned, and pressed subject to the conditions in (1) and (2) above.

SECTION 507. TOBACCO, TOILET ARTICLES, ETC. 

Beneficiaries receiving hospital treatment or domiciliary care in 

Veterans' Administration hospitals or homes, and patients receiving hospital treatment in contract private, municipal or State, or other

Government hospitals at the expense of the Veterans' Administration, who are in receipt of less than $20 monthly from any source, may supplied the following articles and services, in whole or in part as determined needed: Smoking tobacco, cigarettes, pipes, matches, shoe polish, postage stamps, writing paper and letter envelopes, hair combs and brushes, tooth brushes, denture brushes, tooth paste, safety razors, safety-razor blades, shaving brushes, shaving cream or shaving soap, and barber service.

SECTION 508. VETERANS SOJOURNING OR RESIDING ABROAD 

No person is entitled to receive domiciliary, medical, or hospital care, including treatment, who resides outside of the continental limits of the United States or its Territories or possessions, except that in the discretion of the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs necessary hospital care, including medical treatment, may be furnished to veterans who are citizens of the United States and who are temporarily sojourning or residing abroad, for disabilities due to war service in the armed forces of the United States (38 U. S. C. ch. 12, Veterans Regulations).

SECTION 509. OUT-PATIENT TREATMENT 

Out-patient treatment, medical or dental, including necessary medicines, prosthetic appliances, and other supplies, may be afforded veterans for service-connected diseases or injuries, or for non-serviceconnected conditions associated with and held to be aggravating a service-connected disability. Out-patient treatment may also be rendered to persons pursuing a course of vocational training authorized by Public Law 16, Seventy-eighth Congress, March 24, 1943, as amended, who are in need of treatment to avoid interruption of training. Treatment may be given at a Veterans' Administration field station or authorization may be secured for treatment by a physician or dentist in the applicant's home community. Where necessary, treatment may be authorized at the applicant's residence. Application for out-patient treatment may be made in person or by mail on 

Veterans' Administration Form 10-2827, Application for Out-Patient Treatment. 

In order to improve the service to eligible veterans in need of treatment on an out-patient basis, in sections of the country where no Veterans' Administration facilities are feasibly available programs have been worked out so that "home town care," with a free choice of doctors will be available. Plans of two types have been developed for furnishing medical services, the essential difference between the plans being that in one the Veterans' Administration pays the physicians direct for services rendered and in the other the physicians are paid by the State Medical Services who in turn bill the Veterans' Administration for services rendered under contract. This system will eliminate travel on the part of the veteran and take a heavy load off Veterans' Administration hospitals.

As a companion service to the home town medical care program, prescription service is available on a "home town basis" from private pharmacies. Pharmaceutical associations of 46 States, the District of Columbia, and Hawaii have contracts to supply this service through

retail pharmacies. In the other two States contracts are in effect with individual pharmacies. Like the medical contracts, the arrangements with pharmacists apply only to veterans with service-connected disabilities.

These programs have been developed under the authority contained in section 1500 of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, which affords the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs authority to enter into contracts or agreements with private or public agencies, or persons, for necessary services, including personal services, as he may deem practicable (38 U. S. C. 697).

SECTION 510. DENTAL TREATMENT 

Dental treatment may be granted for service-connected dental or oral disabilities whether compensable or otherwise or for a dental condition, not service-connected but medically determined to be aggravating disability from an associated systemic disorder that is either service-connected or not service-connected. 

Dental treatment may also be authorized for veterans receiving domiciliary care who require dental treatment and veterans pursuing a course of vocational training authorized by Public Law 16, Seventyeighth Congress, March 24, 1943, as amended, who require dental treatment to prevent interruption of training. 

SECTION 511. ORTHOPEDIC AND PROSTHETIC APPLIANCES 

Artificial limbs and other prosthetic and orthopedic appliances of a permanent type may be furnished to: (a) Out-patients entitled to and in need of such appliances for a disease or injury which is service-connected or for an associated condition, not attributed to military or naval service, but held to be aggravating the disability from a service-connected disease or injury (adjunct treatment); (b) Hospitalized patients, when medically held needed for a service-connected condition, or an associated disease or injury held to be aggravating disability from a service-connected disorder (adjunct treatment), or a disease or injury not attributed to military service for which hospitalization has been authorized, or for a condition not service-connected that is associated with and held to be aggravating disability from the disease or injury for which hospitalization was authorized; (c) Domiciled members, when medically held needed for a serviceconnected condition, or for a disease or injury not service-connected I but held to be aggravating disability from a service-connected condition (adjunct treatment), or when medically determined necessary as an incident of domiciliary care; and (d) Persons pursuing a course of training under Public Law 16, Seventy-eighth Congress, March 24, 1943, as amended, when medically determined as essential to prevent interruption of such training. 

The orthopedic and prosthetic appliances available for persons enumerated above include artificial limbs, artificial eyes, braces, orthopedic shoes, trusses, crutches, canes, stump socks, hearing devices and batteries therefor, elastic stockings or belts, eye lenses and frames, cosmetic appliances necessary because of disfigurement of the face 81200°-48-5

or hands, and special clothing made necessary through wearing an appliance.

Orthopedic and prosthetic appliances may be repaired or replaced when necessitated in medical judgment because of wear, loss not due to negligence of the beneficiary, or for other sufficient reasons. 

In order to provide an efficient system for the repairing of prosthetic appliances the Veterans' Administration issues service appliance cards to eligible veterans. These cards are used as follows:

Prosthetic Service Card, Veterans' Administration Form 10–2501, is issued to those veterans wearing an artificial limb or limbs who are entitled to repairs at Government expense. These cards upon presenta. tion to any limb manufacturer or repair shop in any section of the United States, will entitle the veteran to immediate repair service up to a limit of $35 without making it necessary for him to obtain prior authority from the Veterans' Administration. These cards do not constitute authority for the purchase of new appliances. The use of this card will enable the veteran to go direct to the prosthetic manufacturer for needed repairs to his prosthesis, thus providing immediate service, and will effect a saving of the veterans' time by reducing travel to field stations of the Veterans' Administration for authority to have repairs made.

Prosthetic Service Card (Aids for the Blind), Veterans' Adminis tration Form 10-2501b, is issued to those blinded veterans who have been furnished equipment by the Veterans' Administration to aid them in overcoming their handicap of blindness. This card, upon presentation to any repair shop in any section of the United States will entitle the veteran to immediate repair service up to a limit of $20 without making it necessary for him to obtain prior authority from the Veterans' Administration. 

Prosthetic Service Card (Hearing Aids), Veterans' Administration 

Form 10-2501c, will be issued upon request to veterans who have established service-connected deafness. This card may be mailed with the hearing aid to the manufacturer of the particular aid to be repaired and will entitle the veteran to service for repair and replacement of parts up to a limit of $25 plus mailing costs, without prior authority from the Veterans' Administration. 

An artificial limb or other appliance (braces, etc., for support of a part in which function has been lost or much impaired) may be supplied or repaired when medically determined necessary for any retired officer or enlisted man of the Army, Navy, Marine Corps, or Coast Guard who lost a limb or the use thereof through injury or disease incurred or contracted in line of duty in the military or naval service at any time. No commutation in lieu of the artificial limb or other appliance may be paid (38 U. S. C. 706b).

Veterans entitled to prosthetic appliances will be furnished such fitting and training, including institutional training, in the use of the appliances as may be necessary, in a Veterans' Administration facility, other training institution, or by out-patient treatment, including necessary expenses to and from their homes to the hospital or training institution (38 U. S. C. 693d). 

Public Law 729, Eightieth Congress, June 19, 1948, authorizes to be appropriated annually to the Veterans' Administration and to remain available until expended the sum of $1,000,000 for prosthetic research,

including all forms of prosthetic and orthopedic appliances and sen sory devices. The Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is authorized by this act to make available the results of his investigations to private or public institutions or agencies and to individuals in order that the unique investigative materials and research data in the possession of the Government may result in improved prosthetic appliances for all disabled persons.

SECTION 512. GUIDE DOGS AND MECHANICAL AND ELECTRONIC EQUIPMENT FOR BLIND VETERANS 

Blind veterans entitled to compensation for a service-connected disability may be furnished trained seeing-eye or guide dogs. In addition they may be supplied the necessary travel expenses to and from their places of residence to the point where adjustment to the seeing-eye or guide dog is available and meals and lodging during the period of adjustment, provided they are required to be away from their usual places of residence during the period of adjustment. 

Mechanical and electronic equipment considered as aiding in overcoming the handicap of blindness may also be provided such beneficiaries (38 U. S. C. 251).

Application for a guide dog should be made by the veteran or his representative on Veterans' Administration Form 10-2630, Application for Guide Dog. 

SECTION 513. INSTALLATION OF RESPIRATION APPARATUS 

Resuscitation and respiration apparatus of the iron-lung type have been installed in many Veterans' Administration hospitals and centers. The number of these stationary-type respirators will be increased from time to time as need develops. Portable respirators are located in each branch area for emergency use and for use in transporting patients.

SECTION 514. HOSPITALIZATION OF VETERANS OF NATIONS ALLIED WITH THE UNITED STATES 

In consideration of reciprocal services extended to the United States, the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is authorized, upon request of the proper officials of the government of any nation allied or associated with the United States in World War II, upon a reimbursement basis, to furnish to discharged members of the military or naval forces of any such government, medical, surgical, and dental treatment, hospital care, transportation and traveling expenses, prosthetic appliances, education, training, or other similar benefits authorized by the laws of such nation for its veterans, and services required in extending such benefits.

The provisions of section 202 (14), World War Veterans' Act, 1924 

(38 U. S. C. 488), are extended and confined to those governments allied with the United States in the war since December 7, 1941, and prior to July 26, 1947. Section 202 (14) authorizes the furnishing under certain specified conditions of medical, surgical, and hospital services to discharged members of the military and naval forces of those governments which were associated with the United States during World War I.

Hospitalization in a Veterans' Administration hospital will not be afforded except in emergencies, unless there are available beds surplus to the need of veterans of this country. However, the Administrator may contract for necessary services in private, State, and other Government hospitals (38 U. Š. C. 488a).

SECTION 515. AUTOMOBILES AND OTHER CONVEYANCES FOR DISABLED VETERANS Veterans of World War II who are entitled to compensation for the loss, or loss of use, of one or both legs at or above the ankle under laws administered by the Veterans' Administration, may be provided with automobiles or other conveyances, at a cost per vehicle or conveyance not to exceed $1,600, including equipment with such special attachments and devices as the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs may deem necessary. Application and adjudication must be made by June 

30, 1949. For this purpose $41,500,000 has been appropriated. No part of this appropriation may be used for the repair, maintenance, or replacement of the automobile or other conveyance and no veteran may be given an automobile or other conveyance until it is established to the satisfaction of the Administrator that the veteran will be able to operate the vehicle in a manner consistent with his own safety and with the safety of others and will be licensed to operate it by the State of his residence or other proper licensing authority. Payment for the automobile or other conveyance will be made by the Veterans' Administration to the seller from whom the veteran is purchasing under sales agreement between the seller and the veteran (38 U. S. C. 

252; Public Laws 663, 79th Cong., Aug. 8, 1946; 161, 80th Cong., July 

3, 1947; 271, 80th Cong., July 30, 1947; 785, 80th Cong., June 25, 1948; 

904, 80th Cong., Aug. 13, 1948).

Application should be made on Veterans' Administration Form 4502. Except in the case of retired officers this application should be forwarded to the regional office of the Veterans' Administration having jurisdiction over the compensation claim file of the veteran. Retired officers should forward their applications to the Veterans' 

Administration, Washington 25, D. C.

SECTION 516. EXEMPTION FROM ADMISSIONS TAX 

No tax will be imposed in the case of admission free of charge of a hospitalized member of the military, naval, or air forces of the United States, or of a person hospitalized as a veteran by the Federal Government in Federal, State, municipal, private, or other hospital or institution, except when the member or veteran is on leave or furlough. This exemption from payment of the admissions tax is effective August 1, 1948, and is subject to regulations prescribed by the 

Commissioner of Internal Revenue with the approval of the Secretary of the Treasury (26 U. S. C. 1700 (a) (1), amended by Public 

Law 706, 80th Cong., June 19, 1948).

SECTION 517. SPECIALLY ADAPTED HOUSING UNITS 

Any person who served in the active military or naval service of the United States and who is entitled to compensation under the provisions of Veterans Regulation 1 (a), as amended, for permanent and

total service-connected disability due to spinal-cord disease or injury with paralysis of the legs and lower part of the body, may obtain assistance in acquiring a suitable housing unit with special fixtures or movable facilities made necessary by the nature of his disability, and necessary land therefor. This assistance will be afforded subject to the provisions and limitations of Veterans Regulation 1 (a), as amended, part IX, and under regulations prescribed by the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs, the regulations to include, among other things, provisions requiring findings that (a) it is medically feasible for the veteran to reside in the proposed housing unit and in the proposed locality; (b) the proposed housing unit bears a proper relation to the veteran's present and anticipated income and expenses; and (c) the nature and condition of the proposed housing unit are such as to be suitable to the veteran's needs for dwelling purposes (38 U. S. C. 701, ch. 12, Veterans Regulations, as amended by Public Law 

702, 80th Cong., June 19, 1948).

The assistance will be limited in the case of any veteran to one housing unit, and necessary land therefor; will not exceed $10,000 (as the 

Government's contribution) in any one case, and will be afforded under one of the following plans, at the option of the veteran : (a) where the veteran elects to construct a housing unit on land to be acquired by him, the Administrator will pay not to exceed 50 percent of the total cost to the veteran of (1) the housing unit and (2) the necessary land upon which it is to be situated; (b) where the veteran elects to construct a housing unit on land acquired by him prior to application for assistance, the Administrator will pay not to exceed the smaller of the following sums: (1) 50 percent of the total cost to the veteran of the housing unit and the land necessary for the housing unit, or (2) 50 percent of the cost to the veteran of the housing unit plus the full amount of the unpaid balance, if any, of the cost to the veteran of the land necessary for the housing unit; (c) where the veteran elects to remodel a dwelling, which is not adapted to the requirements of his disability, acquired by him prior to application for assistance, the Administrator will pay not to exceed the total of (1) 50 percent of the cost to the veteran of such remodeling, plus (2) the smaller of the following sums: (A) 50 percent of the cost to the veteran of the dwelling and the necessary land upon which it is situated, or (B) the full amount of the unpaid balance, if any, of the cost to the veteran of the dwelling and the necessary land upon which it is situated; and (d) where the veteran has acquired a suitable housing unit, the Administrator will pay not to exceed the smaller of the following sums: (1) 50 percent of the cost to the veteran of the housing unit and the necessary land upon which it is situated, or (2) the full amount of the unpaid balance, if any, of the cost to the veteran of the housing unit and the necessary land upon which it is situated. 

Model plans and specifications of suitable housing units may be furnished without cost to veterans eligible for assistance. by No person who accepts the benefits described in this section will reason thereof be denied benefits under title III of the Servicemen's 

Readjustment Act. (See secs. 732–748.)

Applications for assistance should be made on VA Form 4555, which will be furnished to the veteran or his representative upon request. 

The application should be executed by the veteran and forwarded direct to the regional office where his claims folder is located. If no claim has been filed for benefits from the Veterans' Administration, the application should be forwarded to Central Office, Veterans' Administration, Washington 25, D. C.

SECTION 601. BURIAL EXPENSES 

When a veteran of any war, discharged under conditions other than dishonorable, a veteran of any war in receipt of pension or compensation, a veteran discharged from the Army, Navy, Marine Corps, or Coast Guard for disability incurred in line of duty, or a veteran of the Army, Navy, Marine Corps, or Coast Guard in receipt of compensation for service-connected disability dies after discharge, provision is made for payment of burial and funeral expenses and transportation of the body (including preparation of the body) to the place of burial, in a sum not exceeding $150.

No deduction is made from the burial allowance because of any contribution from any source toward the burial and funeral (including transportation) unless the amount of expenses incurred is covered by the amount actually paid for burial and funeral (including transportation) purposes by a State, county, or other political subdivision, workmen's compensation commission, State industrial accident board, employer, burial association, or Federal agency. No claim can be allowed for more than the difference between the entire amount of the expenses incurred, and the amount paid by any or all of the foregoing agencies or organizations. A cash contribution made by a burial association to any person other than the person rendering burial and funeral services will not cause denial of or reduction in the amount of burial allowance otherwise payable. Any contributions made by lodge, union, fraternal organization, society or beneficial organization or insurance company by reason of the death of a veteran will not constitute a bar to the payment of the statutory burial allowance, except where such funds would revert to the funds of the lodge, union, fraternal organization, beneficial association or organization. The law cannot be construed to cause payment of burial allowance or any part thereof in any case where a specific provision is otherwise made for payment of expenses of funeral, transportation, 63

and interment under any other act (par. II, Veterans Regulation No. 

9 (a); 38 U. S. C., ch. 12, Veterans Regulations).

Where a veteran dies in a Veterans' Administration center, hospital, or other institution to which properly admitted under authority of the Veterans' Administration within the continental limits of the United States, the Veterans' Administration will assume the actual cost (not to exceed $150) of funeral and burial or cremation and transport the body or cremated remains to the place of burial or inurnment within the continental limits of the United States or to the place of burial or inurnment in Alaska if the veteran was a resident of Alaska and had been brought to the United States as a beneficiary of the Veterans' 

Administration for hospital or domiciliary care. Where a veteran dies while hospitalized under the authority of the Veterans' Admin istration in a Territory or possession of the United States, the Veterans' Administration will assume the actual cost (not to exceed $150) of funeral and burial or cremation and transport. the body or cremated remains to the place of burial or inurnment within the Territory or possession (par. III, Veterans Regulation No. 9 (a); 38 U. S. C., ch. 12, Veterans Regulations).

When death occurs in a Veterans' Administration center, hospital, or other institution to which properly admitted under authority of the Veterans' Administration within the continental limits of the United States and burial is to be made outside the continental limits (except Alaska as already mentioned), transportation will be allowed to the port of embarkation, or to the border limits of the United States when burial is in Canada or Mexico.

When a person while traveling under proper prior authorization and at the expense of the Veterans' Administration, either to or from a specified destination, for the purpose of examination, treatment, or care, dies en route, burial, funeral, and transportation expenses will be provided in all respects as though death occurred while properly hospitalized by the Veterans' Administration.

Reimbursement shall be allowed for burial, funeral, and transportation expenses in the case of a discharged or rejected draftee; a member of the National Guard who reported to camp in answer to the President's call for World War I service, but who, when medically examined, was not finally accepted for active military service; or a veteran discharged under conditions other than dishonorable from a period of service other than war service who dies in an institution to which properly admitted for hospital or domiciliary care under authority of the Veterans' Administration in the same manner as for a war veteran.

A veteran member or patient of a Veterans' Administration hospital, domiciliary activity of a center, or an institution to which properly admitted under authority of the Veterans' Administration who dies away from such installation from which he has been granted a pass or a leave of absence of not to exceed 72 hours or who has been absent without leave from such installation for not to exceed 24 hours, may be considered to have died in a Veterans' Administration installation within the meaning of the law and regulations governing the payment of burial expenses.

SECTION 602. ELIGIBILITY PROVISIONS (A) GENERAL 

The term "veteran of any war," for purpose of allowance for funeral, burial, and transportation expenses, incurred in behalf of deceased veterans where death occurred on or subsequent to March 20, 1933, 

(1) World War II, any person discharged or released from active duty under conditions other than dishonorable, or retired, who served in the active military or naval service of the United 

States on or after December 7, 1941, and before 12 o'clock noon, December 31, 1946: Provided, That the term "active military or naval service" as used herein shall include active duty as a member of the Women's Army Auxiliary Corps, Women's Army Corps, Women's Reserve of the Navy and Marine Corps, and the Women's 

Reserve of the Coast Guard. 

(2) World War I, any officer, enlisted man, member of the

Army Nurse Corps (female), Navy Nurse Corps (female), discharged under conditions other than dishonorable, or retired, who was employed in the active military or naval service of the 

United States on or after April 6, 1917, and before November 

12, 1918: Provided, however, That if the person was serving in the United States military forces in Russia the ending date will be extended through April 1, 1920.

Where death occurs on or after March 28, 1934, and the other eligibility requirements are met, the character of discharge will not bar payment of the burial allowance if the veteran was in receipt of pension, compensation, or retirement pay at the time of his death unless the person was discharged or dismissed by reason of the sentence of a general court martial, or discharged on the ground that he was a conscientious objector who refused to perform military duty or refused to wear the uniform or otherwise comply with lawful orders of competent military authority, or as a deserter, or in the case of an officer where his resignation is accepted for the good of the service. These conditions bar entitlement to burial allowance based upon the period of service from which the veteran was so discharged or dismissed. 

In the case of peacetime veterans who were discharged or retired for disability but who were not in receipt of compensation for serviceconnected disability at time of death, and where death occurred on or after October 5, 1940, the official records of the service department showing that the disability on account of which the veteran was discharged or separated from his service was incurred in line of duty will be accepted for the purpose of determining eligibility to the burial allowance, notwithstanding the fact that the Veterans' Administration has made a determination in connection with a claim for monetary benefits that the disability was incurred not in line of duty. 

Limitation as to time for filing and perfecting claim 0 (1) All claims for reimbursement or direct payment of burial and funeral expenses and transportation of the body must be filed within 2 years subsequent to the date of permanent burial or cremation of the veteran by the person entitled or by some person acting for him; e. g., burial or cremation on December 1, 1944, claim filed De-

cember 1, 1946, is timely filed. In the event the claimant's application is not complete at the time of original submission, the claimant and the person acting for him, if any, will be notified of the evidence necessary to complete the application, and if such evidence is not received within 1 year from the date of the request therefor no allowance may be paid; provided that if within the 2-year period from date of permanent burial or cremation, the claim is disallowed because the evidence to complete it was not received within 1 year from request therefor, and a new claim (formal or informal) is filed within such 2-year period, the claimant will be notified of the evidence necessary to complete the claim, and if such evidence is received within 1 year from the date of request therefor the allowance may be paid if the claimant is otherwise entitled. (2) Where the death or burial of a veteran occurred on or after

March 20, 1933, and claim for burial allowance was not filed, or was filed after the expiration of the regulatory period, or was filed within the regulatory period and disallowed because the evidence necessary to complete the claim was not timely filed, or was filed within the regulatory period and disallowed in whole or in part because of a payment made by a lodge, union, fraternal organization, society, beneficial organization, insurance company, or because of a cash contribution made by a burial association to any person other than the person rendering burial and funeral services, a claim (formal or informal) filed within 2 years after October 17, 1940, or on or before October 17, 1942, shall be adjudicated under the laws and regulations governing such allowance. (3) Where a claim for the statutory burial allowance, based on the death of a veteran on or after March 20, 1933, has been denied for any of the reasons outlined above, and such claim was otherwise payable under then existing law and regulations, such claim may be reconsidered under present existing law and regulations upon receipt, on or before October 17, 1942, of written request therefor from the claimant or his representative. 

(B) EFFECT OF FORFEITURE OF BENEFITS BY VETERANS 

Forfeiture of benefits by a veteran under the provisions of section 504, World War Veterans Act, as amended, or section 15 of Public, No. 2, Seventy-third Congress, shall not preclude payment of the statutory burial allowance where the death of the veteran occurred on or subsequent to October 17, 1940 (sec. 9, Public, No. 866, 76th Cong., October 17, 1940; 38 U. S. C. 555a).

SECTION 603. FLAGS, 

An American flag, with which to drape the casket of any veteran of a war, discharged under conditions other than dishonorable, or of a person discharged under conditions other than dishonorable from the 

United States Army, Navy, Marine Corps, or Coast Guard, after serving at least one enlistment, or discharged for disability incurred in line of duty, may be issued on application by relatives or undertakers to postmasters at any county-seat post office or any other post office designated by the Veterans' Administration, or to the nearest office of the Veterans' Administration. Such flag shall be given to the

next of kin after burial (par. I, Veterans Regulation No. 9 (a); 38

U. S. C., ch. 12, Veterans Regulations). A flag may also be issued by the above agencies in the case of any person who has died while in the military or naval service of the United States after May 27, 1941, and prior to the termination of World War II, to the nearest relative of such person, or to such other person as the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs deems most appropriate, if no person is otherwise entitled to receive a flag of the United States used at the funeral of the deceased person (Public Law 187, 78th Cong., November 22, 1943).

Application for a flag under either of the foregoing provisions should be made on Veterans Administration Form 11-2008-Application for 

United States Flag for Burial Purposes-for memorial purposes.

Reimbursement will not be made for flags privately purchased by relatives, friends, or other persons, nor will flags be issued to undertakers, organizations, or individuals to replace flags loaned or donated by them.

SECTION 604. HEADSTONES 

Headstones or markers will be furnished for the unmarked graves of all members of the armed forces of the United States dying in the service, or former members whose last service terminated honorably; and for all unmarked graves in post and national cemeteries. If a veteran is buried in a private cemetery, application for a headstone or marker must be made on Quartermaster General Form 623 and submitted to the Quartermaster General, Department of the Army, Washington, D. C. If a veteran is buried in a national cemetery, no application is required. Where burial is in a Veterans' Administration cemetery, the manager of the station will submit application to the Quartermaster General.

Headstones will be shipped freight prepaid by the Government to the railroad station or steamboat landing nearest the cemetery where the veteran is buried (act of February 28, 1929, title II, 45 Stat. 1375; act of July 1, 1948, Public Law 871, 80th Cong.).

SECTION 605. BURIAL IN CEMETERIES MAINTAINED ON VETERANS' ADMINISTRATION RESERVATIONS 

The following persons are entitled to burial in cemeteries maintained on Veterans' Administration reservations: (1) Any member or patient who dies in a Veterans' Administration installation while receiving hospital or domiciliary care. (2) Veterans who die in the immediate vicinity of an installation having a cemetery whose bodies are unclaimed and whose relatives cannot be located, and burial expenses are payable under Veterans' 

Administration regulations.

SECTION 606. BURIAL IN NATIONAL CEMETERIES 

Burial in national cemeteries of the remains of the following classes of persons is authorized under such regulations as the Secretary of the Army may prescribe: (a) Any member or former member of the armed forces of the United States whose last service terminated honorably, by death or otherwise; (b) any citizen of the United States who, during any war in which the United States has been or may hereafter be engaged, served in the armed forces of any government allied with the United States during such war, and whose last service terminated honorably, by death or otherwise; and (c) the wife, husband, widow, widower, minor child, and, in the discretion of the Secretary of the Army, unmarried adult child of any of the persons enumerated in (a) and (b) herein. The remains of those persons enumerated in (c), above, may, in the discretion of the Secretary of the Army, be removed from a national cemetery proper and interred in the post section of a national cemetery or in a post cemetery if, upon death, the related member of the armed forces of the United States or allied government is not buried in the same or an adjoining grave site. 

Persons who were members of the Cabinet of the President of the United States at any time during the period between April 6, 1917, and 

November 11, 1918, may also be buried in any national cemetery, if the interment is without cost to the United States. The term "widow" includes the widow of any members of the armed forces of the United States lost or buried at sea or officially determined to be permanently absent in a status of missing or missing in action (Public Law 526, 80th Cong., May 14, 1948).

SECTION 608. DISPOSITION OF PERSONAL EFFECTS OF PERSONS DYING AT VETERANS' ADMINISTRATION HOMES OR HOSPITALS Veterans' Administration Form 10-P-10, Application for Hospital 

Treatment or Domiciliary Care, contains a provision for designating thereon the person or persons whom the veteran desires to receive his personal funds and effects in case of death and if he dies while receiving hospital treatment or domiciliary care, such funds and effects ordinarily will be delivered to the person so designated for disposition to the person or persons ultimately entitled thereto. 

If a veteran dies in a Veterans' Administration field station without having executed Form 10-P-10, and leaves a will, the funds and effects of such person may be turned over to the executor of the will. If the deceased had made no will such property, upon demand, may be turned over to a qualified administrator for disposition in accordance with the laws of the State in which the deceased person was domiciled. 

Personal effects which remain unclaimed for 90 days after death of the veteran may be sold. Any unclaimed funds and the proceeds of any effects sold as unclaimed are deposited in the general post fund, subject to reclamation by or on behalf of any person entitled thereto within 5 years after notice of sale.

Whenever any veteran (admitted as a veteran) dies in any Veterans' Administration hospital, center, or home or in any Federal, State, or private hospital or other institution while being furnished care or treatment therein by the Veterans' Administration, without leaving any will and leaving no spouse, heirs, or next of kin entitled to his personal property, all such property, including money and choses in action, vests in and becomes the property of the United States as trustee for the sole use and benefit of the general post fund. Assets of the deceased veteran deposited in this fund are subject to valid claims of creditors presented to the Veterans' Administration within 1 year from date of death of the veteran or otherwise as provided by any applicable law, and any heir, next of kin, legatee, or other person found to be legally entitled to the personal property of the veteran may claim same within 5 years from date of the veteran's death or if claimant is under legal disability, within 5 years from date of removal of such legal disability (act of June 25, 1910, as amended by Public 

Law 382, 77th Cong., Dec. 26, 1941, 38 U. S. C. 17).

Personal property left by any person at a Veterans' Administration field station, after a certain length of time and after due notice, will be sold and any person or persons rightfully entitled to the proceeds may make claim therefor at any time within 5 years (Public, No. 734, 75th Cong., June 25, 1938, 38 U. S. C. 16-16J).

PART J. SPECIAL PRISONER OF WAR COMPENSATION 798. War Claims Act of 1948__ Part A. Vocational Rehabilitation for Disabled Veterans SECTION 701. GENERAL 

143 The purpose of vocational rehabilitation is to restore employability lost by virtue of a handicap due to disability incurred in, or aggravated by, service on or after September 16, 1940, and prior to July | 26, 1947. 

This program, under the direction of the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs, was authorized by the act of March 24, 1943, Public Law 16, Seventy-eighth Congress (38 U. S. C. 701, ch. 12, Veterans Regula tions), which amended Public, No. 2, Seventy-third Congress, March 

20, 1933, to include vocational training as a benefit for disabled veter ans of World War II, and added part VII to Veterans Regulation 1 

(a), as amended. This legislation has since been amended and liberalized by the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, June 22, 1944, as amended (38 U. S. C. 693g, 697c, 697f, 697g, ch. 12, Veterans Regulations), and by Public Law 338, Eightieth Congress, August 4, 1947; by Public Law 411, Eightieth 

Congress, February 14, 1948, and Public Law 512, Eightieth Congress, May 4, 1948. 81200°-48-

Vocational rehabilitation under Public Law 16, Seventy-eighth 

Congress, subject to the provisions and limitations of part VII, Veterans Regulation 1 (a), as amended, and often referred to as "part VII benefits," is provided exclusively for disabled veterans of World War II who are suffering from a service-connected disability for which compensation is payable under laws administered by the Veterans' Administration, or would be but for receipt of retirement pay, and who are in need of vocational rehabilitation to overcome the handicap of such disability.

Vocational rehabilitation is to be distinguished from education or training under Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, subject to the provisions and limitations of part VIII, Veterans Regulation 1 

(a), as amended, and often referred to as "part VIII benefits," available to both disabled and nondisabled veterans of World War II who meet the eligibility requirements on the basis of service. (See part B, "Education and Training of Veterans.")

The Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is authorized to prescribe and provide suitable training for disabled veterans who meet the eligibility requirements for vocational rehabilitation or “part VII benefits," and such training, designed to fit the veteran for employment, may be in an educational institution or in training on the job in a business firm or industrial establishment in or near the veteran's place of residence.

SECTION 702. BASIC ELIGIBILITY REQUIREMENTS 

(a) Active military or naval service in the armed forces of the United States at any time on or after September 16, 1940, and prior to July 26, 1947, or service as described in (e) below. (b) Discharge or release from active service under conditions other than dishonorable and under conditions other than those specified in section 300, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, June 22, 1944, set forth in section 202, or a status as described in (f) below. (c) Service-connected compensable disability due to World War II service. (d) Need for vocational rehabilitation to overcome handicap of such disability. (e) Active military or naval service in the armed forces of any government allied with the United States in World War II in the same manner and to the same extent as those who served in the active military and naval service of the United States, provided: (1) That such person shall have been a citizen of the United States at the time of his entrance into the active service, and (2) that such person is a resident of the United States at the time of filing claim for such benefits and (3) that such person has not applied for and received the same or similar benefits from the government of the nation in whose active military or naval service he served. (f) A person while on terminal leave or a person hospitalized pending final discharge may be afforded vocational rehabilitation training under this legislation (except that no subsistence allowance shall be paid in such cases) subject to all the statutory provisions pertaining to eligibility except actual discharge or release from active service. 

Vocational rehabilitation of persons disabled in industry or otherwise under State-approved plans.-Veterans who are not eligible for

vocational rehabilitation by the Veterans' Administration by reason of the fact that they do not have a compensable service-connected disability based upon World War II service or do not otherwise meet the eligibility requirements for such vocational rehabilitation, may, nevertheless be entitled to vocational rehabilitation as a disabled individual or a war-disabled civilian under a State plan for vocational rehabilitation. For complete information concerning any vocationalrehabilitation program established by the State of his legal residence, the veteran should communicate with the social security agency in his 

State (Public Law 113, 78th Cong., July 6, 1943, 29 U. S. C. 31-41).

SECTION 703. APPLICATION Form 7-1900, Disabled Veteran's Application for Vocational Re habilitation, will be furnished by the Veterans' Administration to all disabled veterans of World War II who have applied for, or are receiving compensation, retirement, or retainer pay, and to disabled veterans on terminal leave or hospitalized pending final discharge who wish to apply for vocational rehabilitation. 

Form 7-1900 should be filled out and returned to the Veterans' Administration regional office having the veteran's records. If there are no records, the application should be sent to the Veterans' Administration office having jurisdiction over the area in which the veteran expects to reside. If the veteran has been discharged or released from the service and if he has not filed claim for compensation, he should also fill out Form 8-526, Application for Compensation, and send it with Form 7-1900.

The Veterans' Administration will notify the veteran when and where to report for personal interview with a vocational adviser and if asked to report outside of the community or city in which he resides, provision will be made for the expense of transportation, meals, and lodging for the time he remains at the regional office or guidance center.

Application, formal or informal, by a person who served in the active military or naval service of a government allied with the United States should be sent to the Registration and Research Service, Office of Assistant Administrator for Vocational Rehabilitation and

Education, Veterans' Administration, Washington 25, D. C. Form 

7-1900 should be accompanied by evidence to establish active military or naval service in the armed forces of the allied government on which claim is based and to establish American citizenship at time of entrance into such service.

A veteran on terminal leave should attach to Form 7-1900 a certified or photostatic copy of the authorization placing him on terminal leave. A veteran hospitalized pending final discharge should attach to Form 7-1900 a statement by the proper official of the station or hospital showing he is in active service and is receiving treatment, and the probable date of his discharge.

SECTION 704. LENGTH OF TRAINING 

The disabled veteran is given vocational rehabilitation training for such period of time as may be necessary to restore employability, regardless of the length of his military service.

No course of training in excess of four calendar years may be approved, except with the approval of the Administrator, nor may any training be afforded beyond July 25, 1956. 

SECTION 705. FACILITIES FOR VOCATIONAL REHABILITATION 

The Veterans' Administration may utilize and extend its present facilities, utilize those of other governmental agencies, as well as those maintained by joint Federal and State contribution, or contract with public or private institutions or establishments for such additional training facilities as may be suitable and necessary. (See part B, section 722, Facilities for Education or Training.) SECTION 706. VOCATIONAL ADVISEMENT AND GUIDANCE 

The disabled veteran is given professional counsel and guidance in the selection of an employment objective and such suitable courses of education or training as will enable him to overcome his handicap and restore employability lost by reason of such handicap. 

The selection of an occupation in which rehabilitation will be effected is based upon consideration of the individual veteran's education, vocational experience, abilities, personal desires, and present disability, and contemplates that the vocational training to be provided will supply the necessary occupational information and develop the proper skills to afford the disabled person a well-rounded knowledge of and the ability to perform all the skills, job operations, and work tasks which are essential to meeting employment requirements in the chosen occupation. 

SECTION 707. SUBSISTENCE ALLOWANCE WHILE TRAINING 

Each veteran pursuing a course of vocational training continues to receive the full amount of compensation, retirement pay, or other benefit to which he may be entitled. In addition, while pursuing a course of vocational rehabilitation and for 2 months after his employability is determined he is paid a subsistence allowance each month. 

Generally, this is at the rate of $65 per month, if he has no dependents, or $90 per month, if he has one or more dependents. However, if he is pursuing a course of full-time institutional training the rate is $75 per month, if he has no dependents, $105 per month, if he has one dependent, or $120 per month, if he has two or more dependents. If he is pursuing a course of institutional on-farm training or other combination course he is paid the basic rate of $65 or $90 per month, according to his dependency status, plus an additional amount based upon the relationship between the institutional part of his course and a full-time institutional course. The additional amount is prorated to the difference between the basic rate and the increased rate payable to a full-time institutional trainee of similar dependency status. 

Veterans pursuing courses of vocational rehabilitation training are paid a minimum monthly amount if the amount of their subsistence allowance plus the compensation they receive for disability does not equal such amount. The minimum amounts are: For persons with less than 30-percent service-connected disability, $105 per month, if without dependents, or $115 per month if they have a dependent, plus the

following amounts for additional dependents: $10 for one child, $7 for each additional child, and $15 for a dependent parent; for persons with a disability of 30 percent or more, $115 per month, if without dependents, or $135 per month if they have a dependent, plus the following amounts for additional dependents: $20 for one child, $15 for each additional child, and $15 for a dependent parent. 

If the veteran is pursuing a course of vocational rehabilitation which consists of on-the-job training for which the veteran receives wages or compensation, the amount of subsistence allowance is reduced to an amount considered equitable and just by the Administrator. The amount of compensation or retirement pay, however, is not reduced. 

Subsistence allowance is not paid to a veteran pursuing a course of training while on terminal leave or while hospitalized pending final discharge from the armed services.

SECTION 708. BENEFITS FOR INJURY WHILE IN TRAINING 

Where a trainee suffers an injury, or aggravation of an injury, as a result of the pursuit of a course of vocational rehabilitation, and not the result of his or her own willful misconduct, and such injury or aggravation results in additional disability to or death of such person, benefits will be awarded on the same basis as if the disability, aggravation, or death had been incurred in war service. Application for this benefit must be made within 2 years after the injury was suffered or such death occurred.

SECTION 709. RULES, REGULATIONS, AND PENALTIES 

The Administrator is authorized to make such rules and regulations as may be deemed necessary in order to promote good conduct and cooperation on the part of trainees. Penalties for breach of such rules and regulations may extend to a forfeiture by the offender for a period of 3 months of such portion of the training pay as will leave him not less than the amount of the monthly compensation or retire- Iment pay to which such person is entitled for service-connected disability, and such penalties may also extend to permanent discontinuance of training.

SECTION 710. LEAVES OF ABSENCE 

Leaves of absence may be granted trainees, when, in the opinion of the Administrator, such leaves do not materially interfere with courses of vocational rehabilitation. During such a leave, the trainee will be considered to be pursuing his course of vocational rehabilitation and will continue to receive subsistence allowance. Such leaves are not to exceed 30 days in any consecutive 12 months, except in exceptional circumstances as determined by the Administrator.

SECTION 711. INTERRUPTION OF TRAINING 

A veteran once entered in vocational rehabilitation training will be 1 expected to continue through to completion of his training program without interruption insofar as it is possible for him to do so. 

When training is interrupted by the closing of school for the summer i vacation or if by reason of illness or other good and sufficient cause

training is interrupted for a stated period of time through no fault of the veteran he will be reentered in training when he so desires even though there has been a reduction in his disability rating to less than compensable degree.

SECTION 712. LOANS TO TRAINEES 

The Administrator may make advancement, not exceeding $100 in any case, to persons commencing or undertaking courses of vocational rehabilitation, the loan to bear no interest and to be reimbursed in such installments as may be determined by the Administrator, by proper deductions from any future payments of compensation or retirement pay.

SECTION 713. TUITION, MEDICAL CARE, TREATMENT, AND SUPPLIES 

Full tuition and necessary books and supplies incident to training and all equipment and supplies required in training on the job will be at Government expense. In addition to medical care, treatment, hospitalization, orthopedic and prosthetic appliances otherwise authorized, the Administrator is authorized to provide such care, treatment and supplies as may be necessary to accomplish the purposes of vocational rehabilitation and enable the veteran to continue training whether or not the disease or injury is of service origin.

SECTION 714. RETENTION OF BOOKS, SUPPLIES, OR EQUIPMENT FURNISHED TRAINEE OR STUDENT 

Any books, supplies, or equipment furnished a trainee or student shall be deemed released to him except that if he fail because of fault on his part to complete the course of training or education he may be required to return any or all of such books, supplies, or equipment not actually expended or to repay the reasonable value thereof. turned books, supplies, or equipment may be turned in to educational or training institutions for credit under such terms as may be approved by the Administrator, or disposed of in such other manner as may be approved by the Administrator.

SECTION 715. CHOICE AND ELECTION OF BENEFITS 

Re- A disabled veteran who has been found eligible for vocational rehabilitation under part VII who is also eligible for education or training under part VIII may elect either benefit or may be provided an approved combination of such courses, provided that the total period of such combined courses may not exceed the maximum period of education or training under the part affording the greater period of eligibility.

A veteran who has been found eligible for education or training under part VIII (see pt. B hereof) and whose entitlement to vocational rehabilitation training under part VII has not been determined may enter a course of education or training under part VIII pending the determination of his rights under part VII.

SECTION 716. EMPLOYMENT PLACEMENT 

The Administrator is authorized to cooperate with and employ the facilities of other governmental and State employment agencies for the purpose of placing in gainful employment disabled veterans who have completed courses of education or training designed to accomplish vocational rehabilitation.

Part B. Education and Training of Veterans SECTION 717. GENERAL 

The purpose of education or training under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, as amended, is to enable returning veterans to I recapture educational opportunities lost by reason of entrance into military service and to provide education or training for veterans in need of educational or occupational readjustment. 1 The Education and Training program, under the direction of the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs, was authorized by title II of the 

Servicemen's Readjustment Act, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth 

Congress, June 22, 1944 (38 U. S. C. 693g, 697c, 701, ch. 12 Veterans 

Regulations) which amended Public, No. 2, Seventy-third Congress, 1 March 20, 1933, to provide education and training for veterans of 

World War II, and added part VIII to Veterans Regulation 1 (a), as amended.

This legislation was amended and liberalized by the enactment of Public Law 268, Seventy-ninth Congress, December 28, 

1945, Public Law 679, Seventy-ninth Congress, August 8, 1946, Public 

Law 377, Eightieth Congress, August 6, 1947, Public Law 411, Eightieth Congress, February 14, 1948, and Public Law 512, Eightieth Congress, May 4, 1948 (38 U. S. C. 697f, 697g, ch. 12 Veterans Regulations).

By section 11 (a), Public Law 190, Seventy-ninth Congress, October 

6, 1945, it was provided that for the purpose of such benefits World War II shall not be considered as terminating in the case of any individual, before the termination of such individual's first period of enlistment or reenlistment contracted within 1 year after the date of the enactment of the Armed Forces Voluntary Recruitment Act of 

1945 (October 6, 1945). This provision had the effect of extending the period of service which may be counted in determining a veteran's total period of entitlement to education or training to include the time covered by such enlistment or reenlistment, even though in all other cases the basic period for service was terminated as of July 25, 1947, by Public Law 239, Eightieth Congress, approved July 25, 1947, which fixed that date as the termination of World War II which is generally applicable in fixing the close of the period within which service must have been rendered.

The benefits of education or training are available to both nondisabled and disabled veterans of World War II who meet the eligibility requirements as to period of service and type of discharge. A disabled veteran who has been found eligible for vocational rehabilitation or "part VII benefits" and is also eligible for education and training or "part VIII benefits" may elect either benefit or may be provided an approved combination of such courses not to exceed the

maximum period of entitlement under the part affording the greater benefit. (See sec. 715, pt. A, Choice and Election of Benefits.) veteran who is eligible for a course of education or training under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, as amended, may take the course of study in any approved educational institution or training establishment in which he chooses to enroll, whether or not it is located. in the State in which he resides.

SECTION 718. BASIC ELIGIBILITY REQUIREMENTS 

(a) Active military or naval service in the armed forces of the United States at any time on or after September 16, 1940, and prior to July 26, 1947, or service as described in (f) below. (b) Discharge or release from active service under conditions other than dishonorable and under conditions other than those specified in section 300, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, June 22, 1944, set forth in chapter II, section 202, or a status described in (g) below. (c) Service of 90 days or more, exclusive of any period during which veteran was assigned for a course of education or training under (a) the Army specialized training program or (b) the Navy college training program, either of which courses was a continuation of his civilian course and was pursued to completion, or (c) as a cadet or midshipman at one of the service academies. The 90 days or less service need not all be within the period September 16, 1940, to end of the war (July 25, 1947), but may include service extending into or beyond such period and two periods of service may be combined to make 90 days; or- (d) Discharge or release from active service by reason of an actual service-incurred injury or disability, in which event service may have been of less than 90 days. (e) Period of eligibility expires unless course initiated not later than 4 years after date of veteran's discharge from service or 4 years after termination of World War II (July 25, 1947), whichever is later. (f) Active military or naval service in the armed forces of any government allied with the United States in World War II in the same manner and to the same extent as those who served in the active military and naval service of the United States, provided (1) that such person shall have been a citizen of the United States at the time of his entrance into the active service, and (2) that such person is a resident of the United States at the time of filing claim for such benefits and (3) that the person has not applied for and received the same or similar benefits from the government of the nation in whose active military or naval service he served. (9) A person while on terminal leave or a person hospitalized pending final discharge may be afforded education or training under this legislation (except that no subsistence allowance shall be paid in such cases) subject to all the statutory provisions pertaining to eligibility except actual discharge or release from active service. Training under these conditions will be charged against the total period of entitlement.

SECTION 719. APPLICATION Form 7-1950, Application for a Course of Education or Training may be filed (1) with the Veterans' Administration regional office nearest the veteran's location, (2) with the regional office having the veteran's records, if any previous claim has been filed, (3) with the regional office in the area in which the approved educational or training institution is located which has accepted the veteran as a student or trainee, or (4) through such institution which will refer the application to the Veterans' Administration for approval of eligibility and necessary financial arrangement for payment of subsistence allowance. 

A certified or photostatic copy of the veteran's record of service furnished at time of discharge or release from active service should be attached to the application. If the veteran was inducted as an enlisted man and later commissioned, or if he had more than one period of service, records for both periods of service should be furnished as period of entitlement to education or training is computed on basis of length of service, both enlisted and commissioned. 

Application, formal or informal, by a person who served in the active military or naval service of a government allied with the United States should be sent to the Registration and Research Serv tice, Office of Assistant Administrator for Vocational Rehabilitation and Education, Veterans' Administration, Washington 25, D. C. 

Form 7-1950 should be accompanied by evidence to establish active military or naval service in the armed forces of the allied government on which claim is based and to establish American citizenship at time of entrance into such service, residence in the United States at time of filing application, and that similar benefits have not been applied for or received from the allied government. 

If the veteran is on terminal leave, a certified or photostatic copy of such authorization should be attached, or if hospitalized pending discharge, a statement by the proper official of the station, post, or hospital in which he is receiving treatment, showing probable date of his discharge.

If the veteran is found eligible, he will be given notification of the exact period of full-time training to which he is entitled under the law and that he may elect his course of training and select an approved institution. Form 7-1953, Certificate of Eligibility and Entitlement, will be used to notify the veteran. The veteran will also be informed that he may use the certificate as evidence of his eligibility for education or training under the law in contacting the institution which he has selected.

SECTION 720. LENGTH OF EDUCATION OR TRAINING 

(a) Education or training, at an approved educational or training institution, for a period of 1 year, plus the time such person was in service on or after September 16, 1940, and before July 26, 1947. (b) No course of education or training may exceed four calendar years. (c) No education or training may be afforded beyond July 25, 1956, except that in case of a first enlistment or reenlistment entered into between October 6, 1945, and October 6, 1946, this limiting period shall be 9 years after discharge therefrom.

(d) Where the period of eligibility ends during a quarter or se mester and after a major portion of such quarter or semester has expired, such period of instruction will be extended to the termination of such unexpired quarter or semester. 

SECTION 721. CHOICE OF COURSES OF EDUCATION OR TRAINING 

The Servicemen's Readjustment Act provides that the veteran, otherwise eligible, is entitled to such course of education or training, full time or the equivalent thereof in part-time training, as he may elect, and at any approved educational or training institution at which he chooses to enroll, whether or not located in the State in which he resides, which will accept or retain him as a student or trainee in any field or branch of knowledge which such institution finds him qualified to undertake or pursue. However, Public Law 862, Eightieth Congress, June 30, 1948, which appropriated funds for the activities of the Veterans' Administration for the fiscal year 1949, limits the use of such funds as follows:

Provided, That no part of this appropriation for education and training under title II of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act, as amended, shall be expended for tuition, fees, or other charges, or for subsistence allowance, for any course elected or commenced by a veteran on or subsequent to July 1, 1948, and which is de termined by the Administrator to be avocational or recreational in character. 

For the purpose of this proviso, education or training for the purpose of teaching a veteran to fly or related aviation courses in connection with his present or contemplated business or occupation shall not be considered avocational or recreational. 

For reasons satisfactory to the Administrator, the veteran may change a course of instruction, and a course of instruction may be discontinued at any time, if it is found by the Administrator that, according to the regularly prescribed standards and practices of the institution, the conduct or progress of the veteran is unsatisfactory. 

Education in foreign countries in approved institutions is permissible on the same basis as for those in the United States. Detailed information, together with a list of approved institutions in any particular country, will be sent upon request addressed to the Foreign 

Operations Division, Office of Vocational Rehabilitation and Educa tion, Veterans' Administration, Washington 25, D. C.

Under the provisions of Public Law 584, Seventy-ninth Congress, approved August 1, 1946, the Department of State is authorized to utilize foreign currencies and credits acquired through the sale of surplus property abroad for programs of student exchange. Veterans of 

World Wars I and II will be given preference in the selection of students receiving scholarships or grants under this program. However, veterans receiving education in foreign countries under the Servicemen's Readjustment Act may be entitled to additional benefits to care for certain costs not provided under that act. See section 810 for further details on this program. 

SECTION 722. FACILITIES FOR EDUCATION OR TRAINING 

Educational or training institutions include all public or private elementary, secondary, and other accredited and approved schools furnishing education for adults, business schools and colleges, scientific and technical institutions, colleges, vocational schools, junior

colleges, teachers' colleges, normal schools, professional schools, universities and other educational institutions, and also include business or other establishments providing apprentice or other training on the job, including those under the supervision of an approved college or university or any State department of education, or any State apprenticeship agency or State board of vocational education, or any State apprenticeship council or the Federal Apprentice Training Service established in accordance with Public Law 308, Seventyfifth Congress, or any agency in the executive branch of the Federal

Government authorized under other laws to supervise such training. 

The term "other training on the job," as used above, includes courses offered by establishments approved by the appropriate agency of the State or the Administrator whenever such courses of training on the job are furnished in accordance with the following provisions: 

1. Any establishment desiring to undertake an on-the-job training program will be required to submit to the appropriate State approving agency a written application setting forth the course of training for each job for which a veteran is to be trained.
