The written application covering the training program will include the following: (a) Title and description of the specific job objective for which the veteran is to be trained. (b) Length of the training period. (c) Schedule listing various operations for major kinds of work or tasks to be learned and showing for each, job operations or work, tasks to be performed, and the approximate length of time to be spent on each operation or task. (d) Wage or salary to be paid at the beginning of the training program, at each successive step in the program, and at the completion of training. (e) Entrance wage or salary paid by the establishment to employees already trained in the kind of work for which the veteran is to be trained. (f) Number of hours of supplemental instructions required. 

2. The appropriate approving agency of the State or the Administrator may approve the application of the establishment when such establishment is found upon investigation to have met or made provision for meeting the following criteria: (a) The training content of the program is adequate to qualify the veteran for appointment to the job for which he is to be trained. (b) There is reasonable certainty that the job for which the veteran is to be trained will be available to him at the end of the training period. (c) The job is one in which progression and appointment to the next higher classification are based upon skills learned through organized training on the job and not on such factors as length of service and normal turn-over. (d) The wages to be paid the veteran for each successive period of training are not less than those customarily paid in the establishment and the community to a learner in the same job and who is not a veteran and are in conformity with State and Federal laws and applicable bargaining agreements. (e) The job customarily requires a period of training of not less than 3 months and not more than 2 years of full-time training.

(ƒ) The length of the training period is no longer than that customarily required by the establishment and other establishments in the community to provide the trainee with the required skills, arrange for the acquiring of job knowledge, technical information, and other facts which the trainee will need to learn in order to become competent on the job for which he is being trained. (g) Provision is made for related instruction for the individual veteran who may need it. (h) There is in the establishment adequate space, equipment, instructional material, and instructor personnel to provide satisfactory training on the job. (i) Adequate records are kept to show the progress made by the veteran toward his job objective and a periodic report showing the conduct and progress made in the course of training on the job will be provided for the Veterans' Administration. (3) Appropriate credit is given the veteran for previous job experience, whether in the military service or elsewhere, his beginning wage adjusted to the level to which such credit advances him and his training period shortened accordingly. No course of training will be considered bona fide if given to a veteran who is already qualified by training and experience for the job objective. (k) A copy of the training program as approved by the State agency is provided to the veteran and to the Veterans' Administration by the employer. (7) Upon completion of the training the veteran is given a certificate by the employer indicating the length and type of training provided and that the veteran has completed the course of training on the job satisfactorily. 

3. The Veterans' Administration is not authorized to award training benefits, if it is found by the Administrator that the course of apprentice training or other training on the job fails to meet the foregoing requirements.

The Administrator is required, insofar as he deems practicable, to utilize existing facilities and services of Federal and State departments and agencies on the basis of mutual agreements with such departments or agencies. From time to time the Administrator is also required to secure from the appropriate agency of each State a list of the educational and training institutions (including industrial establishments), within such jurisdiction, which are qualified and equipped to furnish education or training (including apprenticeship, refresher or retraining and institutional on-farm training), which institutions, together with such additional ones as may be recognized and approved by the Administrator, shall be deemed qualified and approved to furnish education or training to such persons as shall enroll. 

Wherever there are established State apprenticeship agencies expressly charged by State laws to administer apprentice training, whenever possible, the Administrator shall utilize such existing facilities and services in training on the job when such training is of 1 year's duration or more.

The Administrator is authorized to reimburse State and local agencies for expenses incurred in their approval and supervision of job training and in furnishing information concerning educational opportunities available in schools and colleges.

The term "institutional on-farm training" includes any course of instruction approved by the appropriate agency of the State or the Administrator. Such course will be considered a full-time course when it combines (1) organized group instruction in agricultural and @related subjects of at least 200 hours per year (and of at least 8 hours each month) at an educational or training institution, with (2) supervised work experience on a farm or other agricultural establishment. 

To be approved, such a course will be developed with due consideration to the size and character of the farm on which the veteran is to receive his supervised work experience and to the need of the veteran, in the type of farming for which he is training, for proficiency in planning, producing, marketing, farm mechanics, conservation of resources, food conservation, farm financing, farm management, and the keeping of farm and home accounts. The course will, in addition, satisfy the requirements of either of the following: 

1. If the veteran performs part of his course on a farm under his - own control: (a) He will receive not less than 100 hours of individual instruction per year, not less than 50 hours of which will be on such farm (with at least two visits by the instructor to such farm each month). Such individual instruction is to be given by the instructor responsible for the veteran's institutional instruction and will include instruction and home-study assignments in the preparation of budgets, inventories, and statements showing the production, use on the farm, and sale of crops, livestock, and livestock products; (b) He will be assured of control of such farm (whether by ownership, lease, management agreement, or other tenure arrangement) until the completion of his course; and (c) Such farm will be of a size and character which (1) together with the group-instruction part of the course, will occupy the full time of the veteran, (2) will permit instruction in all aspects of the management of a farm of the type for which the veteran is being trained, and (3) if the veteran intends to continue operating such farm at the close of his course, will assure him a satisfactory income under normal conditions. 

2. If the veteran performs part of his course as the employee of another: (a) He will receive, on his employer's farm, not less than 50 hours of individual instruction per year (with at least one visit by the instructor to such farm each month), such individual instruction to be given by the instructor responsible for the veteran's institutional instruction; (b) His employer's farm will be of a size and character which (1) together with the group-instruction part of the course, will occupy the full time of the veteran, and (2) will permit instruction in all aspects of the management of a farm of the type for which the 

1 veteran is being trained. (c) His employer will agree to instruct him in various aspects of farm management in accordance with the training schedule developed for the veteran by his instructor, working in cooperation with his employer.

Where it has been found that a variation in the proportion of hours of group instruction and individual instruction on the farm

will better serve the conditions in a certain area, any program acceptable to the State approving agency which meets the total number of training hours called for in the act (including assembled instruction, individual instruction, and assigned and supervised related home study and supervision in operational skills by the farmer-trainer under the direction of the institution) will be recognized as complying with the requirements of the act.

The Administrator is authorized to contract with approved institutions for such institutional on-farm courses where the Administrator finds that the agreed cost is reasonable and fair. 

SECTION 723. EDUCATION AND VOCATIONAL GUIDANCE 

The Administrator is authorized to arrange for educational and vocational guidance to persons eligible for education and training and to make available, at such intervals as he deems necessary, information respecting the need for general education and for trained personnel in the various crafts, trades, and professions, utilizing to the extent deemed practicable the facilities of other Federal agencies collecting such information.

Education and vocational guidance service is provided by professionally trained counselors at Veterans' Administration offices, and guidance centers located in universities and colleges throughout the country, in order to help the veteran explore his interests, needs, abilities, and capacities, as well as to provide him with information about schools, training and job opportunities, and chances of success in different occupational fields. Such assistance should help the veteran make a wise choice in selecting an occupation and courses for which he is suited and prepared, thus avoiding waste of training time and loss of opportunity for additional periods of training. 

SECTION 724. SUBSISTENCE ALLOWANCE 

While enrolled in and pursuing a course (except a correspondence course) the veteran, upon application to the Administrator, will be paid a subsistence allowance. The subsistence allowance is in addition to pension, compensation, retirement pay, or benefits otherwise payable.

The basic rate of subsistence allowance is $65 per month, if the veteran has no dependents, or $90 per month, if he has one or more dependents. However, if the veteran is pursuing a course of full-time institutional training he will be paid $75 per month, if he has no dependents, or $105 per month, if he has one dependent, or $120 per month, if he has two or more dependents. If he is pursuing a course of part-time institutional training, including a course of institutional on-farm training or other combination course, the additional subsistence allowance is based upon the relationship between the institutional part of the course and a full-time institutional course, the additional amount being prorated to the difference between the basic rate and the increased rate payable to a full-time institutional trainee of similar dependency status.

A person attending a course on a part-time basis or a person receiving compensation from productive labor may be entitled to receive subsistence allowance in an amount less than the full rate, as determined by the Administrator. In no event may the allowance plus compensa

tion received from productive labor (based upon the standard workweek exclusive of overtime) exceed $210 per month if the veteran is without a dependent, $270 per month if he has one dependent, or $290 per month if he has two or more dependents. 

Subsistence allowance is not payable to persons pursuing courses while on terminal leave or while hospitalized awaiting discharge. 

SECTION 725. PAYMENTS TO EDUCATIONAL OR TRAINING INSTITUTIONS 

The Administrator is authorized to pay to the educational or training institution (including the institution offering institutional onfarm training) for each person enrolled in a full-time or part-time course of education or training, the customary cost of tuition, and such laboratory, library, health, infirmary, and other similar fees as are customarily charged, and may pay for books, supplies, equipment, and other necessary expenses (exclusive of board, lodging, other living expenses, and travel) as are generally required for the successful pursuit and completion of the course by other students in the institution. Payments, with respect to any person, may not exceed $500 for an ordinary school year unless the veteran elects to have such customary charges paid in excess of such limitation, in which event there shall be charged against his period of eligibility the proportion of an ordinary school year which such excess bears to $500. 

No payments may be made to institutions, business, or other establishments furnishing apprentice training on the job. Any institution 

I may apply to the Administrator for an adjustment of tuition, and the Administrator, if he finds that the customary tuition charges are insufficient to permit the institution to furnish education or training to eligible veterans, or inadequate compensation therefor, may provide for the payment of such fair and reasonable compensation as will not exceed the estimated cost of teaching personnel and supplies for instruction; and may in like manner readjust such payments from time to time.

SECTION 726. COURSES OF EDUCATION OR TRAINING OF LESS THAN 30 WEEKS 

An eligible veteran may elect to apply for a short intensive postgraduate or training course of less than 30 weeks at an approved educational or training institution at the agreed cost of such course as determined by the Administrator to be reasonable and fair, even though the customary charges are in excess of the rate of $500 for a full-time course for an ordinary school year, but not more than $500 may be paid for any such course.

SECTION 727. COURSES OF EDUCATION OR TRAINING COSTING IN EXCESS OF RATE OF $500 FOR AN ORDINARY SCHOOL YEAR 

Customary charges in excess of the rate of $500 for an ordinary school year may be paid by the Veterans' Administration only when the veteran elects to have such excess costs paid, thereby shortening his period of eligibility. In this event the veteran will be required before entering training to execute Form 7-1950-a, in addition to Form 7-1950.

When the veteran has selected his approved school or training institution he must assume the responsibility for ascertaining the costs before he commences his course of education or training. If the customary charges are in excess of the rate of $500 for an ordinary school year and if he desires the Veterans' Administration to pay such excess, he is required to execute Form 7-1950-a which will be completed by the authorized official of the institution and forwarded to the Veterans' Administration regional office having jurisdiction over the territory in which the institution is located, together with the original certificate of eligibility and other papers required. 

An eligible veteran pursuing a short, intensive postgraduate or training course of less than 30 weeks for which customary or agreed charges are in excess of the rate of $500 for a full-time course for an ordinary school year shall have charged against his period of eligibility the proportion of an ordinary school year (34 weeks or 238 days) which the cost of the course bears to $500. Thus there will be charged against the period of eligibility 1 day for each $2.10 ($500 divided by 

238) of the cost of the course but the total charge against the period of eligibility shall not be less than the number of days a full-time course is pursued.

An eligible veteran pursuing a full-time course for which he elects to have the Veterans' Administration pay in excess of $500 for an ordinary school year shall have charged against his period of eligibility the length of the school year and an additional period of 1 day for each $2.10 of the cost in excess of $500. 

An eligible veteran pursuing a course on a part-time basis who elects to have the Veterans' Administration pay the cost in excess of the rate of $500 for an ordinary school year shall have charged against his period of eligibility the usual charge for a part-time course on the basis of three-fourths, one-half, or one-fourth of a school year and an additional period of 1 day for each $2.10 of that part of the cost in excess of the $500 rate. 

SECTION 728. CORRESPONDENCE COURSES WITHOUT SUBSISTENCE ALLOWANCE 

An eligible veteran may elect to apply for a course of instruction by correspondence without any subsistence allowance with an approved institution at the agreed cost of such course as determined by the Administrator to be reasonable and fair and, if approved, one-fourth of the elapsed time in following such course shall be charged against the veteran's period of eligibility. The total amount payable for a correspondence course or courses for any veteran shall not exceed $500 but this provision does not preclude the use of approved correspondence courses as a part of institutional or job training, subject to the regulations prescribed by the Administrator. 

If the veteran elects to pursue a course of instruction by correspondence only, he will be required to apply on Form 7-1950-b in addition to Form 7-1950.

SECTION 729. RULES, REGULATIONS, AND RESTRICTIONS 

The Administrator of Veterans' Affairs, consistent with and subject to the provisions and limitations of the law itself is authorized to pre-

L scribe and promulgate such rules and regulations as may be necessary to carry out its purposes and provisions.

No department, agency, or officer of the United States, in carrying out the program, shall exercise any supervision or control whatsoever over any State educational agency, or State apprenticeship agency, or any educational or training institution. However, this prohibition does not prevent any department, agency, or officer of the United States from exercising any supervision or control which such department, agency, or officer is authorized, by existing provisions of law, to exercise over any Federal educational or training institution, or prevent the furnishing of education or training under this part in any institution over which supervision or control is exercised by such other department, agency, or officer under authority of existing provisions of law.

SECTION 730. TUITION, MEDICAL TREATMENT, AND SUPPLIES 

Tuition and necessary books and supplies incident to education or training are at Government expense, including laboratory, library, and other fees customarily charged.

The veteran is entitled to such medical treatment as he would otherwise be entitled to by virtue of his military or naval service, or by virtue of the special provisions of the education or training institution in which he is enrolled as a student or trainee for which a health or infirmary fee is customarily charged. 

In case of sickness the veteran may resume his course but total period of entitlement may not be increased. No subsistence allowance may be paid while the veteran is away from training due to sickness except where such periods may be covered by accumulated 30 days annual leave.

SECTION 731. RETENTION OF BOOKS, SUPPLIES, OR EQUIPMENT FURNISHED TRAINEE OR STUDENT 

(See sec. 714, pt. A, Vocational Rehabilitation for Disabled Veterans.)

Part C. Guaranty or Insurance of Loans for Purchase or Con struction of Homes, Farm and Business Property, and for Other Purposes

SECTION 732. INTRODUCTION Title III of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, Public Law 

346 Seventy-eighth Congress, June 22, 1944, as amended by Public

Law 268, Seventy-ninth Congress, December 28, 1945 (38 U. S. C. 

693g, 694–694e, 697c, 697f, 697g), authorizes the Administrator of

Veterans' Affairs to guarantee not to exceed 50 percent of any loan or loans made to eligible veterans of World War II for the purchase, construction, or improvement of homes, farms, and business property, and for other ordinary farming purposes and business enterprises, provided that the aggregate amount of the guaranty which may be extended to any veteran on one or more loans may not exceed $2,000 in the case of non-real-estate loans and $4,000 in the case of real-estate loans, or a prorated portion thereof in combination loans of both types. 81200°-48- -7

Provision is also made whereby any loan eligible for guaranty under this title may, in lieu of such guaranty, be insured by the Administrator against loss to the extent of 15 percent of the principal of the loan but not in excess of the veteran's available guaranty for the type of loan. 

The guaranty or insurance of a loan by the Administrator on behalf of the United States increases the amount of security a veteran can offer and correspondingly increases the amount he can borrow. 

The right to guaranty or insurance of loans will continue until July 25, 1957, affording veterans ample opportunity to purchase property or undertake business or other obligations at an advantageous time, taking into account prevailing prices, business opportunities, and other considerations. Loans may be made for any legitimate purpose prescribed within the limitations of the act and must be used for the purpose for which they are obtained. The purchase price of any property or cost of any construction or improvements must not exceed the reasonable value of the property as determined by an appraiser appointed by the Administrator. Loans made by prescribed lending agencies subject to State or Federal supervision will be automatically guaranteed or insured if the lender and the veteran have consummated a loan in full compliance with the specific provisions of the law and regulations. Loans to be made by lenders other than those supervised by State or Federal agencies may be guaranteed only after prior approval by the Administrator.

The Administrator is authorized to promulgate such rules and regulations as are necessary and appropriate for carrying out the provisions of this title, as amended, and may delegate to subordinate employees authority to issue certificates, or other evidence, of guaranty of loans and to exercise other administrative functions thereunder. 

The loan regulations issued under title III of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, as amended, may be obtained from the nearest office of the Veterans' Administration.

SECTION 733. GENERAL PROVISIONS 

Loans guaranteed or insured under this title are payable under such terms and conditions as may be agreed upon by the lending agency and the veteran, subject to the conditions and limitations of the law and regulations issued pursuant thereto, which include the following provisions of paramount importance: (a) The interest rate on any loan guaranteed or insured wholly or in part may not exceed 4 percent per annum on the unpaid principal balance, except in the case of non-real-estate insured loans. (See sec. 

744.) However, Public Law 901, Eightieth Congress, approved

August 10, 1948, authorizes the Administrator, with the approval of the Secretary of the Treasury, to prescribe by regulation for a higher rate of interest, not in excess of 412 percent per annum, if he finds the loan market demands such action. (6) An amount equivalent to 4 percent of the amount originally guaranteed or insured will be paid to the lender by the Administrator out of available appropriations, to be credited upon the loan. This amount may be treated as a payment on the principal of the loan or, at the election of the borrower, may be used to pay part of the interest during the first year. The veteran is not required to repay this amount.

(c) Loans on real estate are payable in full in not more than 25 years, or in the case of loans on farm realty in not more than 40 years. 

Non-real-estate loans are payable in full in not more than 10 years. 

Nonamortized loans are payable in not more than 5 years and every guaranteed or insured loan is repayable within the economic life of the property securing the loan. (d) Any loan for a term in excess of 5 years must be amortized in accordance with established lending practice so as to require approximately equal periodic payments not less often than annually during the life of the loan, except that such installments may be varied on farm and business loans to allow for seasonal fluctuations of income, and on farm real-estate loans to postpone principal repayments for not more than 2 years from the date of the loan. (e) Any real-estate loan, other than for repairs, alterations, or improvements, must be secured by a first lien or mortgage on the realty, except as to secondary loans. See sec. 742.) (f) A non-real-estate loan, except as to working or other capital, merchandise, good will, and other intangible assets, must be secured by personal property to the extent legal and practicable. (g) The liability of the Government under a guaranty is reduced or increased pro rata with any reduction or increase in the amount of the unpaid portion of the obligation. (h) The debtor or veteran has the right to prepay at any time without premium or fee the entire indebtedness or any part thereof, but not less than the amount of one installment or $100, whichever is less. (i) If the entire guaranty credit of $4,000 is used for a real-estate loan, no guaranty credit for a non-real-estate loan remains available. 

If the entire guaranty credit of $2,000 is used for a non-real-estate loan, no guaranty credit remains available for a real-estate loan. (3) If part of the guaranty credit is used with respect to one type of loan, the amount available for the other type of loan is proportionately reduced. For example, if a guaranty credit of $2,000 is used for a real-estate loan, or 50 percent of the total guaranty available, the amount of guaranty credit for a non-real-estate loan is reduced to $1,000, or 50 percent of the total guaranty available for a non-realestate loan. If $1,000 or 50 percent of the nonrealty guaranty is used, the amount of guaranty credit for a real-estate loan is reduced to $2,000 or 50 percent of the total guaranty available for a real-estate loan. (k) Once the maximum amount of guaranty or insurance entitlement is used by the veteran, no further credit will be available to him, nor will the full or partial payment of an indebtedness permit the granting of any further guaranty or insurance benefit. 

SECTION 734. BASIC ELIGIBILITY REQUIREMENTS 

(a) Active military or naval service in the armed forces of the United States at any time on or after September 16, 1940, and prior to July 26, 1947. (b) Discharge or release from active service under conditions other than dishonorable and under conditions other than those specified in section 300, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, June 22, 1944, as amended, set forth in chapter II, part A, section 202.

(c) Active service of 90 days or more or discharge or release from active service by reason of an injury or disability incurred in service in line of duty. (d) A person who meets the above conditions, save for actual discharge or release from service, may be eligible while on terminal leave or while hospitalized pending final discharge. (e) A person who served in the armed forces of a government allied with the United States in World War II and who at time of entrance into such service was a citizen of the United States and who at time of application is also a resident of the United States, is eligible under the same conditions as set forth above. (f) The period of eligibility will expire July 25, 1957, 10 years after termination of World War II, as fixed for this purpose by Public 

Law 239, Eightieth Congress, July 25, 1947. However, for the purposes of obtaining loan benefits, World War II shall not be considered as terminating, in the case of any individual, before the termination of such individual's first period of enlistment or reenlistment contracted within 1 year after the date of enactment of the Armed Forces 

Voluntary Recruitment Act of 1945, Public Law 190, Seventy-ninth 

Congress, October 6, 1945, section 11 (b) of which amended section 

500, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, June 22, 1944, as amended.

SECTION 735. PROCEDURE FOR OBTAINING CERTIFICATE OF ELIGIBILITY FOR LOAN GUARANTY 

An honorable discharge shall be deemed a certificate of eligibility to apply for a guaranteed or insured loan. A veteran who does not have a discharge certificate or who received a discharge other than honorable may apply to the Administrator for a certificate of. eligi bility, Veterans' Administration Form 4-1870. 

The Administrator will endorse on the original discharge or eligibility certificate the amount and type of guaranty available or used and the amount, if any, remaining. Veterans who have used their guaranty benefit for a loan under the original act and who desire to use all or a part of the additional guaranty available under the amended act should submit their original discharge or separation papers to the nearest office of the Veterans' Administration for endorsement of the amount of guaranty benefit used or reserved, and the amount remaining available for use at some future time.

Photostatic, certified, or other copies of discharge or separation papers will not be acceptable in determining the veterans' eligibility or for endorsement thereon of the guaranty or insurance benefit used or available. A veteran who has lost his original discharge or separation papers should apply to the branch of the service from which he was discharged for a certificate in lieu of discharge or other authenticated copy of his separation papers. 

Where a veteran otherwise eligible for a loan-guaranty benefit has not received a formal discharge for the reason that he is on terminal leave or hospitalized pending final discharge, he may apply for a certificate of eligibility to the nearest office of the Veterans' Administration, submitting authenticated evidence of his status from the branch of the service from which he is to be discharged. Upon re-

ceiving his formal discharge or separation papers he should forward same to the Veterans' Administration for endorsement. 

A certificate of eligibility issued prior to March 1, 1946, and not used should be returned immediately to the Veterans' Administration for issuance of a new certificate of eligibility upon which may be endorsed the amount of guaranty benefit used or available. 

SECTION 736. PROCEDURE FOR OBTAINING GUARANTY OR INSURANCE OF LOANS 

A veteran desiring to obtain guaranty or insurance of a loan should first consult the bank, corporation, firm, lending agency, or person from whom he expects to borrow the money, presenting the lender with his original honorable discharge or other original separation papers evidencing the nature of his discharge and the duration of his active military service. In most instances eligible lenders will have available the necessary information and proper application forms to cover the type of loan desired. If such information is not available, the veteran should consult the nearest representative of the 

Veterans' Administration. 

The veteran may make his own choice of an eligible lender either supervised or nonsupervised. Supervised lenders include any Federal land bank, national bank, State bank, private bank, building and loan association, insurance company, credit union, or mortgage and loan company that is subject to examination and supervision by an agency of the United States or of any State or Territory, including the District of Columbia.

SECTION 737. AUTOMATIC GUARANTY OF LOANS BY SUPERVISED LENDERS 

A loan made by a supervised lender will be automatically guaranteed or insured when made in accordance with the act and regulations and when reported to the regional office of the Veterans' Administration having supervision over the area in which the lender is located, within 30 days following full disbursement, and upon certification of the lender that- (a) The loan has been made in full accordance with the terms. and provisions of the act. (b) The required security has been obtained. There has been full disbursement of the proceeds for an eligible purpose to the veteran borrower, or for his benefit at his direction, and (d) There has been no default thereunder. 

The lender will send to the regional office of the Veterans' Administration, the veteran's original honorable discharge, or separation papers, or certificate of eligibility issued by the Veterans' Administration, the note or other evidence of debt signed by the veteran, the appraiser's report, and the loan closing statement of the lending agency. The Veterans' Administration will endorse the discharge certificate with the amount of guaranty used and remaining to the veteran, and will endorse the note or evidence of debt to show the amount of the guaranty or insurance and its percentage relation to the amount of the loan, or in lieu thereof, will issue a loan guaranty

certificate, Form 4-1899, or certificate of insurance credit, Form 4-1871, which will be attached to the note and returned to the lender by registered mail.

In the majority of cases the supervised lender will be able to satisfy himself as to the applicant's eligibility and as to the amount of guaranty available from an examination of the veteran's original honorable discharge, or certificate of eligibility issued by the Veterans' Administration, and upon receipt of the appraiser's report the lender will proceed to close the loan and pay the funds to the veteran. However, the risk is on the lender and loans may be submitted for prior determination by the Veterans' Administration of the three following requirements which must be finally confirmed by the Administrator: 

1. Veteran's eligibility, 2. Amount of guaranty or insurance credit available to the veteran, and 3. Eligibility of the purpose of the loan. 

Loans in excess of $15,000, to be insured, must be submitted for prior confirmation.

No particular forms have been prescribed for use by supervised lenders. The number and title of the forms required in the processing of loans which may be requisitioned from the nearest office of the Veterans' Administration for use by supervised lenders for guaranty or insurance of loans are:

Form No. 

4-1820 Home Loan Report (white). 

4-1840 Farm Loan Report (green). 

4-1860 

Title Business Loan Report (blue). 

4-1803 Appraisal Reports (home and business realty) (white). 

4-1823 Appraisal-Farm (real estate and nonreal estate) (green). 

4-1845 Appraisal-Non-real-estate business loan (blue). 

4-1868 Report of Delinquent Indebtedness (white). 

4-1874 Claim Under Loan Guaranty (new law, Public 268) (white). 

4-1876 Certificate of Loan Disbursement (white). 

4-1872 

Semiannual Status Report-Insured Loans (white). 

4-1873 Report of Insured Loans Transferred (white). 

4-1875 Claim under Loan Insured (new law, Public 268) (white). 

4-6850 Notice of Default (white). 4-6851 

Notice of Intention to Foreclose (white).

SECTION 738. GUARANTY OF LOANS MADE BY NONSUPERVISED LENDERS 

Loans made by nonsupervised lenders may be guaranteed only after prior approval by the Administrator. After a nonsupervised lender has determined basic eligibility of the veteran and that the loan he contemplates making is eligible under the act and regulations, he will have the veteran fill out the prescribed application for guaranty, obtain a credit or character report, and order an appraisal to determine the reasonable value of the property to be purchased. Upon receipt of satisfactory credit and appraisal reports and determination of insurance requirements and expenses incident to closing of a loan, the lender will prepare the loan closing statement, Form 4-1861, revised, which must be signed by the veteran and the lender. The lender will then forward to the regional office of the Veterans' Administration having supervision of the area in which the loan is to be made the veteran's original honorable discharge, or other separation papers, or the certificate of eligibility issued to the veteran by the

Veterans' Administration, the application for loan guaranty, appraisal and credit reports, loan-closing statement, copy of the offer or contract of purchase and any other documents required under the regulations for the particular type of loan. 

Upon approval of a loan the Administrator will issue a certificate of approval, Form 4-1867, and forward it to the lender with necessary instructions to close the loan. The original honorable discharge, other separation papers, or certificate of eligibility, will also be returned to the lender who will return same to the veteran. After the loan is closed and fully disbursed, the lender within 30 days thereafter will forward to the Veterans' Administration Form 4-1861, loan closing statement and note or other evidence of indebtedness, if the lender desires to have the amount of the guaranty endorsed thereon, or in the case of a loan in excess of $1,000 for new construction, alteration, improvement, or repairs, a letter from the designated appraiser that the work has been completed in accordance with plans and specifications. The Administrator will then issue the evidence of guaranty or insurance credit, as the case may be, either by endorsing the note or other evidence of indebtedness as guaranteed and its percentage relation to the amount of the loan, or in lieu thereof, will issue a loan guaranty certificate, Form 4-1899, or certificate of insurance credit, Form 4-1871, which will be attached to the note and returned to the lender by registered mail.

The number and title of prescribed forms for use by nonsupervised lenders which may be requisitioned from the nearest office of the Veterans' Administration are:

Form No. 

Title 

4-1802 Application for Home Loan Guaranty (white). 

4-1822 Application for Farm Loan Guaranty (green). 4-1842 

Application for Business Loan Guaranty (blue). 

4-1803 Appraisal Reports (Home and Business Realty) (white). 

4-1823 Appraisal Report, Farm (green). 4-1845 4-1861 4-1868 Appraisal-Non-real-estate business loans (blue).

Loan Closing Statement (Revised) (white). 

Report of Delinquent Indebtedness (white). 

4-1874 Claim Under Guaranty (new law, Public 268) (white). 

4-6850 Notice of default (white). 

4-6851 Notice of intention to foreclose.

SECTION 739. GUARANTY OF LOANS FOR PURCHASE OR CONSTRUC- TION OF HOMES 

Any loan made to a veteran under this title, the proceeds of which are to be used for purchasing residential property or constructing a dwelling to be occupied as his home or for the purpose of making repairs, alterations, or improvements in property owned by him and occupied as his home, is automatically guaranteed if made pursuant to the provisions of this title, including the following: (1) That the proceeds of such loan will be used for payment of the property purchased or constructed or improved; (2) That the contemplated terms of payment required in any mortgage to be given in part payment of the purchase price or the construction cost bear a proper relation to the veteran's present and anticipated income and expenses; and that the nature and condition of the property is such as to be suitable for dwelling purposes; and

(3) That the price paid or to be paid by the veteran for such property or for the cost of construction, repairs, or alterations does not exceed the reasonable value thereof as determined by proper appraisal made by an appraiser designated by the Administrator.

Loans made for construction of homes may include the cost of the lot on which the veteran intends to build. So-called "closing costs" which are locally required to be paid by the purchaser or lienor may be included as part of the loan. However, no commission for making the loan may be charged against the debtor or the proceeds of the loan. 

SECTION 740. GUARANTY OF LOANS FOR PURCHASE OF FARMS AND FARM EQUIPMENT 

Any loan made to a veteran under title III the proceeds of which are to be used for purchasing any lands, buildings, livestock, equipment, machinery, supplies or implements, or for repairing, altering, constructing or improving any land, equipment, or building, including the farmhouse, to be used in farming operations conducted by the veteran involving production in excess of his own needs, or for working capital requirements necessary for such operations, or to purchase stock in a cooperative association where the purchase of such stock is required by Federal statute as an incident to obtaining the loan, is automatically guaranteed if made pursuant to the provisions of title III, including the following: 

(1) That the proceeds of such loan will be used for any such purposes in connection with bona fide farming operations conducted by the applicant; (2) That such property will be useful in and reasonably necessary for efficiently conducting such operations; (3) That the ability and experience of the veteran, and the nature of the proposed farming operations to be conducted by him, are such that there is a reasonable likelihood that such operations will be successful; and (4) That the purchase price paid or to be paid by the veteran for such property does not exceed the reasonable value thereof as determined by proper appraisal made by an appraiser designated by the Administrator.

Veterans who desire to engage in farming or who seek counsel and guidance on agricultural matters are advised that general information on the subject is available through the Cooperative Agricultural 

Extension Service of the Department of Agriculture. County agri cultural agents are located in all agricultural counties and are assisted by advisory committees composed of local farmers and businessmen who are familiar with agricultural conditions in the various sections of the country. These agents will provide the veteran with general information as to appropriate types of farming, availablity and costs of farm lands, what constitutes customary partnership agreements and leasing agreements prevailing in the community, what kind of farming set-ups are required for success in a particular community and approximate investment required for such set-ups, as well as other valuable information requisite to success in farming operations.

County agricultural agents are also in a position to advise as to what kind of credit is needed, where and how to get such credit, what

are safe margins of indebtedness, and the institutions, public or private, making loans in the community. No particular lender is recommended and no specific form of credit is urged, leaving the veteran free to choose the source of credit that meets his needs. The address of the county agent may be obtained from the local post office or courthouse.

Any person who is a veteran eligible for the benefits of this title, and who is found by the Secretary of Agriculture, by reason of his ability and experience, including training as a vocational trainee, to be likely to carry out successfully undertakings required of him under a loan which may be made under the Bankhead-Jones Farm Tenant Act, shall be eligible for the benefits of such act to the same extent as if he were a farm tenant. (See sec. 749, pt. D.)

SECTION 741. GUARANTY OF LOANS FOR PURCHASE OF BUSINESS PROPERTY OR EQUIPMENT AND TO ENGAGE IN BUSINESS 

Any loan made to a veteran under this title, the proceeds of which are to be used for the purpose of engaging in business or pursuing a gainful occupation, or for the cost of acquiring for such purpose land, buildings, supplies, equipment, machinery, tools, inventory, stock in trade, or for the cost of the construction, repair, alteration, or improvement of any realty or personalty used for such purpose, or to provide the funds needed for working capital, is automatically guaranteed if made pursuant to the provisions of this title, including the following: (1) That the proceeds of such loan will be used for any of the specified purposes in connection with bona fide pursuit of gainful occupation by the veteran; (2) That such property will be useful in and reasonably necessary for the efficient and successful pursuit of such business or occupation; (3) That the ability and experience of the veteran, and the conditions under which he proposes to pursue such business or occupation, are such that there is a reasonable likelihood that he will be successful in the pursuit of such business or occupation; and (4) That the purchase price paid or to be paid by the veteran for such property, or the cost of such construction, alterations, or improvements, does not exceed the reasonable value thereof as determined by proper appraisal made by an appraiser designated by the Administrator.

SECTION 742. SECONDARY LOANS 

In any case wherein a principal loan, for any of the purposes stated in this title is approved by another Federal agency to be made or guaranteed or insured by it pursuant to applicable law and regulations, and the veteran is in need of a second loan to cover the remainder of the purchase price or cost, or a part thereof, the Administrator, subject otherwise to the provisions of title III, may guarantee the full amount of the second loan to the extent of $4,000 on real estate loans and $2,000 on non-real-estate loans if these amounts of guaranty are available: Provided, That such second loan shall not exceed 20 percent of the purchase price or cost and that such purchase price or cost

does not exceed the reasonable value as determined by an appraiser designated by the Administrator: And provided further, That regulations to be promulgated jointly by the Administrator and the head of such agency may provide for servicing of both loans by such agency and for refinancing of the principal loan to include any unpaid portion of the secondary loan with accrued interest, if any, after the curtail ment thereon equals twice the amount of the secondary loan. 

A second loan is eligible for guaranty or insurance under this title only if the proceeds thereof are used concurrently with and as part of the same transaction which is partially financed through the proceeds of the primary loan, or by continuing the primary loan in effect by assumption or otherwise.

SECTION 743. LOANS TO REFINANCE DELINQUENT INDEBTEDNESS 

Any loan made to a veteran, the proceeds of which are to be used to refinance any indebtedness of the veteran which is secured of record on property to be used or occupied by the veteran as a home or for farming purposes, or indebtedness incurred by him in the pursuit of a gainful occupation which he is pursuing or which he proposes in good faith to pursue, or any delinquent taxes or assessments on such property or business, is automatically guaranteed if made pursuant to the provisions of this title, including the following: (1) Such loan became in default or the delinquency occurred not later than 10 years after the termination of the war (July 25, 1947, for this purpose); (2) Such refinancing will aid the veteran in his economic readjustment; and (3) The amount of the guranteed loan does not exceed the reasonable value of the property or business, as determined by proper appraisal made by an appraiser designated by the Administrator. (4) No loan shall be made to refinance delinquent indebtedness less than 60 days in default without a written statement from the holder of the delinquent obligation or other evidence satisfactory to the Administrator that it is delinquent. (5) Any loan proposed to be made to refinance a delinquent obligation which was incurred within 1 year of the date of the application for refinancing shall be referred in advance to the Administrator with a report of the proposed borrower's income and expenses, and his affidavit stating the purchase price of the property purchased with the proceeds of the delinquent loan. The Administrator may hold any such case to be ineligible if it appears the guaranty or insurance of such refinancing would effect an evasion of any of the limitations imposed by the act. (6) No loan guaranteed or insured by the Administrator under title III of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act may be refinanced under this provision of the act.

SECTION 744. INSURANCE OF LOANS 

Any loans eligible for guaranty under the provisions of this title, when made or purchased by any financial institution subject to examination and supervision by an agency of the United States or of any State or Territory, including the District of Columbia, may, in lieu of

such guaranty, be insured by the Administrator under an agreement whereby he will reimburse any such institution for losses incurred on such loan up to 15 percent of the aggregate of loans so made or purchased by it.

Loans insured under this title shall be made on such other terms, conditions, and restrictions as the Administrator may prescribe, including the following: (a) The amount of insurance credited to the lender's account and charged against the veteran's right of guaranty may not exceed 15 percent of the principal of the loan or the unused portion of the veteran's guaranty, whichever is the lesser amount, subject to the limitation of $4,000 in the case of a real-estate loan and $2,000 in the case of a non-real-estate loan, or a proportionate amount in combination loans of both types. Partial use of either of these credits may be made, reserving the balance of the guaranty for future use. (b) The interest rate on an insured real-estate loan may not exceed 4 percent per annum. (However, see sec. 733 (a) re authority of Administrator to increase interest rate to not in excess of 42 percent.) A higher rate of interest is permissible on nonreal-estate loans but not in excess of a 3-percent discount rate or an equivalent straight interest rate of 5.7 percent per annum on nonamortized loans. (c) An amount equivalent to 4 percent of the amount of the insurance credit will be paid by the Administrator to the lender which may be treated as a payment on the principal of the loan or, at the election of the veteran, may be used to pay part of the interest during the first year. The veteran is not required to repay this amount.

SECTION 745. PROCEDURE ON DEFAULT 

In the event of default in the payment of any loan guaranteed or insured under this title, the holder of the obligation is required to notify the Administrator. The Administrator shall thereupon pay to such holder the amount of the original guaranty, or if the indebtedness has been reduced, not in excess of the pro rata portion of the amount originally guaranteed, and shall be subrogated to the contract and the lien or other rights of the holder to the extent of any sum paid on such guaranty or insured loss, which right shall be junior to the holder's rights as against the debtor or the encumbered property until the holder shall have received the full amount payable under his contract with the debtor. No partial or complete release by a creditor shall impair the rights of the Administrator with respect to the debtor's obligation.

Prior to suit or foreclosure the holder of the obligation shall notify the Administrator of the default, and within 30 days thereafter the Administrator may, at his option, pay the holder of the obligation the unpaid balance of the obligation plus accrued interest and receive an assignment of the loan and security. The Administrator may establish the date, not later than the date of judgment and decree of foreclosure or sale, upon which accrual of interest or charges shall cease. 

It is the policy of the Administrator, consistent with the beneficial nature of the legislation, to encourage lenders to extend all possible

forbearance or leniency in the event a deserving veteran becomes unable to fully meet his obligations under a loan. The lender is given broad discretion in extending the terms of repayment where it appears that the veteran will be able to meet a reduced schedule of payments and the Administrator will in general concur in any plan which appears likely to work out successfully for the veteran, except that no loan may be extended beyond the maximum period of maturity prescribed for loans of its class. Any amounts paid by the Administrator on account of the liabilities of any veteran guaranteed or insured under the provisions of this title shall constitute a debt owing to the United States by such veteran. 

SECTION 746. POWERS OF ADMINISTRATOR OF VETERANS' AFFAIRS 

(a) With respect to matters arising by reason of title III as now or hereafter amended and, notwithstanding the provisions of any other law, the Administrator may- (1) Sue and be sued in his official capacity in any court of competent jurisdiction, State or Federal; (2) Subject to specífic limitations in this act, consent to the modification, with respect to rate of interest, time of payment of principal or interest or any portion thereof, security or other provisions of any note, contract, mortgage, or other instrument securing a loan which has been guaranteed or insured hereunder; (3) Pay, or compromise, any claim on, or arising because of, any such guaranty or insurance; (4) Pay, compromise, waive, or release any right, title, claim, lien, or demand, however acquired, including any equity or any right of redemption; (5) Purchase at any sale, public or private, upon such terms and for such prices as he determines to be reasonable, and take title to, property, real, personal or mixed; and similarly sell, at public or private sale, exchange, assign, convey, or otherwise dispose of any such property; and (6) Complete, administer, operate, obtain, and pay for insurance on, and maintain, renovate, repair, modernize, lease or otherwise deal with any property acquired or held pursuant to this title: Provided, That the acquisition of any such property shall not deprive any State or political subdivision thereof of its civil or criminal jurisdiction of, on, or over such property (including power to tax) or impair the rights under the State or local law of any persons on such property. (b) The powers by this section granted may be exercised by the Administrator without regard to any other provisions of law not enacted expressly in limitation hereof, which otherwise would govern the expenditure of public funds: Provided, That section 3709 of the

Revised Statutes shall apply to any contract for services or supplies on account of any property acquired pursuant to this section if the amount of such contract exceeds $1,000. (c) The financial transactions of the Administrator incident to, or arising out of, the guaranty of loans pursuant to this title, and the acquisition, management, and disposition of property, real, personal, or mixed as incident to such activities and pursuant to this section, shall be final and conclusive upon all officers of the Government.

SECTION 747. INCONTESTABILITY OF EVIDENCE OF GUARANTY OR INSURANCE 

Public Law 864, Eightieth Congress, approved July 1, 1948, added a new section to title III of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act which reads as follows: 

SEC. 511. Any evidence of guaranty or insurance issued by the Administrator shall be conclusive evidence of the eligibility of the loan for guaranty or insurance under the provisions of this title and of the amount of such guaranty or insurance, except that nothing in this section shall preclude the Administrator from establishing, as against the original lender, defenses based on fraud or material misrepresentation, and except that the Administrator shall not, by reason of anything contained in this section, be barred from establishing, by regulations in force at the date of such issuance or disbursement, whichever is the earlier, partial defenses to the amount payable on the guaranty or insurance. 

SECTION 748. SECONDARY MARKET FOR LOANS 

Public Law 864, Eightieth Congress, approved July 1, 1948, as amended by Public Law 901, Eightieth Congress, approved August 10, 1948, provided for the establishment of a Federal National Mortgage 

Association in the Reconstruction Finance Corporation. Such association is authorized, among other things, to purchase from the original lender mortgages which are guaranteed after April 30, 1948, under section 501, 502, or 505 (a) of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act, where the original principal obligation does not exceed $10,000 for each family-dwelling unit covered by the mortgage. The lender offering such mortgages for sale to the Association cannot offer a mortgage if the unpaid principal amount of such mortgage, when added to the aggregate amount paid by the Association for all mortgages purchased from such lender, will exceed 50 percent of the original principal amount of all mortgages made by such lender which would be eligible for sale to the Association. The lender must certify that the housing with respect to which the offered mortgage was made meets the construction standards prescribed for the insurance of mortgages on the same class of housing under the National Housing Act, as amended (the so-called FHA minimum standards).

Part D. Loans Administered by Federal Agencies Other Than the Veterans' Administration SECTION 749. FARM OWNERSHIP LOANS UNDER THE BANKHEAD- JONES FARM TENANT ACT 

The Bankhead-Jones Farm Tenant Act, as amended, administered by the Farmers Home Administration, provides for two types of loans, one a direct loan by the Administration, the other a loan insured by the Administration. County committees of three members examine loan applications. Direct loans bear interest at 4 percent per annum and are amortized over a period of 40 years. Insured loans are likewise amortized over a period of 40 years but they bear interest at 3 percent per annum plus an insurance charge, payable to the Secretary of Agriculture, of 1 percent per annum. Either type loan is made to enable the borrower to purchase a family-size farm, to purchase additional acreage to enlarge a farm to family size or to enable the borrower to adapt his farm to a changing type of agriculture. Insured loans may

not be in excess of 90 percent of the reasonable value of the farm. 

Under the act, veterans who served in the armed forces of the United States in any war against another nation are given a preference for either type loan. Veterans with pensionable disabilities may obtain loans for farms of a size suited to their abilities. In addition, section 

505 (b) of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944 provides that any veteran eligible for the benefits of title III of such act, who is found by the Secretary of Agriculture, by reason of his ability and experience, including training as a vocational trainee, to be likely to carry out successfully undertakings required of him under the Bankhead-Jones Act, shall be eligible for the benefits of such act to the same extent as if he were a farm tenant. 

SECTION 750. INSURED LOANS FOR CONSTRUCTION OR REPAIR OF HOMES, FEDERAL HOUSING ADMINISTRATION 

The Federal Housing Administration insures certain types of loans made in conformity with the provisions of the National Housing Act, as amended, and regulations thereunder. Included in the types of loans insured are loans for the modernization or improvement of existing structures; loans for the purchase or construction of residential housing consisting of four units or less; loans for the construction of multiple-unit dwellings; loans to finance industrial manufacture of housing; mortgages on the sale of Government housing; and mortgages involving large-scale modernized site construction or erection of housing. Veterans of World War II and their immediate families are granted a preference or priority of opportunity in the occupancy of mortgaged property constructed under loans insured under various provisions of title VI of the act. Where mortgages are insured to finance a project of a nonprofit cooperative ownership housing corporation whose membership consists primarily of World War II veterans, the amount of the mortgage may be equal to 95 percent of the 

Federal Housing Commissioner's estimate of December 31, 1947 replacement costs of comparable properties as distinguished from a loan limitation of 90 percent of the Commissioner's estimate of the value of such housing when completed, if it is to be occupied by other than veterans (Public Law 901, 80th Cong., Aug. 10, 1948). 

Part E. Readjustment Allowances for Former Members of the 

Armed Forces Who Are Unemployed

SECTION 751. INTRODUCTION The Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, as amended (38 U. S. C. 

696-696m, 697c, 697f), provides in title V for payment of readjustment allowances to former members of the armed forces who are unemployed. Such allowances may be paid to eligible veterans for each week of unemployment, not to exceed 52 weeks, provided unemployment occurs after September 3, 1944, and not later than 2 years after discharge or release from the service, or July 25, 1947, whichever is the later date, and provided that no readjustment allowance may be paid for any week commencing after July 25, 1952. However, in case of a first enlistment or reenlistment contracted between October 6, 1945, and October 6, 1946, the qualifying period is extended so that

the week of unemployment may occur within 2 years after termination of such enlistment (Public Law 190, 79th Cong., Oct. 6, 1945; 38

SECTION 752. BASIC ELIGIBILITY REQUIREMENTS 

(A) Active military or naval service at any time after September 16, 1940, and prior to July 26, 1947, or service as described in (E) below. (B) Discharge or release from active service under conditions other than dishonorable and under conditions other than those contained in section 300, Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, as amended, set forth in section 202, chapter II. 

(C) Active service of 90 days or more, unless discharged for injury or disability incurred in service in line of duty. (D) As to any week (a period of seven consecutive calendar days) of unemployment for which allowance is claimed the person- (a) Must be residing in United States at time of claim. (b) Must be completely unemployed, having performed no service and received no wages, or if partially unemployed in that services have been performed for less than a full workweek, wages for the week must be less than the allowance plus $3. (c) Must be registered with and continue to report to a public employment office in accordance with its regulations. (d) Must be able to work and be available for suitable work. 

Failure to comply with requirement which is due to illness or disability which occurs after commencement of period of continuous unemployment will not render a person ineligible. (E) A person who served in the armed forces of a government allied with the United States in World War II, and who at time of entrance into such service was a citizen of the United States, is eligible under the same conditions as set forth above, provided he has not applied for or received the same or similar benefits from the government, of an allied nation with which he served. 

SECTION 753. DISQUALIFICATIONS 

(A) Leaves suitable work voluntarily, without good cause. (B) Suspended or discharged for misconduct in course of employment. (C) Failure without good cause to apply for suitable work to which referred by a public employment office. (D) Failure without good cause to accept suitable work when offered. (E) Without good cause, does not attend an available free training course, as required by regulations, which would appear to hold out a prospect of reemployment within a reasonable period not too far in the future. Failure to accept education under part VII or part VIII, Veterans Regulation No. 1 (a), as amended, as distinguished from training, does not disqualify a claimant from receiving an allowance. (F) Unemployment due to a stoppage of work which exists because of a labor dispute at the factory, establishment, or premises at which veteran is or was last employed, except where it is shown that veteran is not participating in or directly interested in the labor dispute which

causes the stoppage of work and does not belong to a grade or class of workers of which, immediately before the commencement of the stoppage, there were members employed at the premises where the stoppage occurs, any of whom are participating in or directly interested in the dispute. If in any case separate branches of work, which are commonly conducted as separate business in separate premises, are conducted in separate departments of the same premises, each such department will be deemed to be a separate factory, establishment, or other premises.

SECTION 754. PENALTIES RESULTING FROM DISQUALIFICATIONS 

A claimant disqualified under any provision of (A) to (E), above, will be disqualified to receive any readjustment allowance for the week in which the cause of his disqualification occurred and for not more than four immediately following weeks.

In cases of successive disqualifications, the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs may extend the period of disqualification for such additional period as he may prescribe, but not to exceed eight additional weeks in the case of any one disqualification.

A claimant disqualified under the provision of (F), above, will be disqualified to receive any readjustment allowance for any week with respect to which his unemployment is due to stoppage of work under the circumstances therein prescribed.

SECTION 755. TERMS DEFINED 

In determining the suitability of work or the existence of good cause with respect to a claimant, the conditions and standards prescribed by the unemployment compensation laws of the State in which he files claim will govern and as to any State having no applicable statute the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is authorized to prescribe the conditions and standards for applicants. No work will be deemed suitable for an individual if the position offered is vacant due directly to a strike, lock-out, or other labor dispute, or the wages, hours, or other conditions of the work offered are substantially less favorable to him than those prevailing for similar work in the locality. 

Wages mean all remuneration for services from whatever sources, including commissions and bonuses and the cash value of all remuneration in any medium other than cash. 

SECTION 756. PERIOD DURING WHICH ALLOWANCES PAYABLE 

Readjustment allowances are payable for each week of unemployment not to exceed a total of 52 weeks. The number of weeks of allowance to which each eligible veteran will be entitled will be determined by the length of the period of his active service after September 16, 1940, and prior to July 26, 1947, except that in the case of a first enlistment or reenlistment between October 6, 1945, and October 6, 1946, this period is extended to the end of such enlistment (Public Law 190, 79th 

Cong., Oct. 6, 1945; 38 U. S. C. 696). Credit will not be given for time lost by reason of unauthorized absence for which the service person has forfeited pay.

For each calendar month or major fraction thereof (16 days or more) of active service during the period prescribed above, the vet-

eran will be entitled to 4 weeks of allowances. For the qualifying 

90 days' period of service, the veteran will be entitled to 8 weeks for each month.. As stated above, however, the total number of weeks may not exceed 52, but benefits need not be drawn for consecutive weeks.

No readjustment allowance will be paid for any period for which the veteran receives subsistence allowance while pursuing a course of vocational rehabilitation under Public Law 16, Seventy-eighth Con gress, March 24, 1943, as amended, or while pursuing a course of education or training under title II of the Servicemen's Readjust ment Act of 1944 (pt. VII, pt. VIII, Veterans Regulation 1 (a), as amended; 38 U. S. C., ch. 12, Veterans Regulations).

SECTION 757. AMOUNT OF ALLOWANCE AND PAYMENT 

The allowance for a week will be $20 less that part of the wages payable to the veteran which is in excess of $3. Where the allowance is not a multiple of $1, it will be computed to the next highest multiple of $1.

Readjustment allowances will be paid at the intervals prescribed by the unemployment compensation law of the State in which claim. was made and if none are so prescribed they will be paid at such reasonable intervals as may be determined by the Administrator. 

Any allowances remaining unpaid upon the death of a claimant shall not be considered a part of the assets of the estate of the claimant, or liable for the payment of his debts, or subject to any administration of his estate, and the Administrator may make payment thereof to such person or persons he finds most equitably entitled thereto. 

SECTION 758. SELF-EMPLOYED VETERANS 

A veteran who meets the eligibility requirements and who is residing in the United States if self-employed for profit in an independent establishment, trade, business, profession, or other vocation, upon showing that he has been fully engaged in such self-employment and that his net earnings in a trade, business, profession, or vocation have been less than $100 in the previous calendar month, is entitled to receive, subject to the limitations as to time and amount, the difference (adjusted to the next highest multiple of $1), between $100 and his net earnings for such month.

The eligibility requirements under section 752 (D) and disqualifications shown above do not apply to self-employed veterans. 

Payment to such veterans will be made by the Administrator at the time and in the manner other payments are made directly to veterans by the Administrator.

SECTION 759. UNEMPLOYED VETERANS COVERED BY RAILROAD UNEMPLOYMENT INSURANCE ACT, AS AMENDED 

In the case of any veteran eligible under the provisions of title V who either at the time of application for the benefits herein provided is a "qualified employee" as defined in section 3 of the Railroad Unemployment Insurance Act, as amended (45 U. S. C. 353), or was last employed prior to such application by an employer as defined in section 81200°-48-

1 (a) of the said act, claim may be made through an office operated by or a facility designated as a free employment office by the Railroad Retirement Board pursuant to the provisions of said act. In such cases, the conditions and standards as to suitability of work or existence of good cause, the intervals for making claim for and payment of benefits, and the administrative and appellate procedures prescribed by or under said act shall govern, if not in conflict with the provisions of this title, the appellate procedures being subject to final appeal to the Administrator. In such cases, a reference in this title to a cooperating State agency shall be deemed to include the Railroad 

Retirement Board. 

SECTION 760. ADMINISTRATIVE PROVISIONS 

The Administrator of Veterans' Affairs is authorized to administer this title and to utilize existing facilities and services of Federal and State departments or agencies on the basis of mutual agreements with such departments or agencies. Such agreements will provide for the filing of claims with the Administrator through established public employment offices and State unemployment compensation agencies and in the processing, adjustment, and determination of claims and payment of the allowances. A representative of the Administrator, who must be a war veteran, discharged from service under honorable conditions and a resident of the State for at least 2 years, will be located in each participating State department or agency. 

The Administrator is authorized to prescribe rules and regulations consistent with the provisions of this title and to require such records and reports as he may find necessary to carry out its purposes. Cooperative rules and regulations relating to the performance by Federal and State departments or agencies of functions under agreements made therewith may be made by him after consultation and advisement with representatives of such departments or agencies. He is authorized to delegate to any officer or employee of his own or of any cooperating department or agency of any State such of his powers and duties other than of prescribing rules and regulations as he may consider necessary and proper to carry out the purposes of this title. 

Allowances paid by cooperating State agencies will be repaid upon certification by the Administrator. The amounts so certified will be paid monthly to the departments, agencies, or individuals designated. 

The Administrator is authorized to certify to the Secretary of the Treasury for payment such sums as he estimates to be necessary to compensate any Federal department or agency for its administrative expenses under this title and to certify to the Federal Security Administrator, the State departments, or agencies participating, and the amount of administrative expense incurred or to be incurred by a State under agreements made, which Administrator will then certify to the Secretary of the Treasury the amount to be paid to each State out of appropriations for the Veterans' Administration.

SECTION 761. APPEAL 

Any claimant whose claim for an allowance has been denied shall be entitled to a fair hearing before an impartial tribunal of the State agency or such other agency as may be designated by the Administrator. The representative of the Administrator located in each

State shall be the final appellate authority in regard to contested claims arising in such State, subject to review by the Administrator. 

SECTION 762. ADJUSTMENT OF DUPLICATE BENEFITS 

Where an allowance is payable to a claimant under this title and where, for the same period, either an allowance or benefit is received under any Federal or State unemployment or disability compensation law, the amount received or accrued from such other source shall be subtracted from the allowance payable under this title (except that this section shall not apply to pension, compensation, or retired pay paid by the Veterans' Administration); and the resulting allowances, if not a multiple of $1, shall be readjusted to the next higher multiple of $1.

SECTION 763. AUTHORITY TO ISSUE SUBPENAS AND INVOKE AID OF COURTS 

The authority to issue subpenas and provisions for invoking aid of the courts of the United States in case of disobedience thereto, to make investigations, and to administer oaths, as contained in title 

III of the act of June 29, 1936 (38 U. S. C. 131-133), shall be ap plicable in the administration of this title.

SECTION 764. PENALTIES 

Any claimant who knowingly accepts an allowance to which he is not entitled shall be ineligible to receive any further allowance under this title.

Whoever, for the purpose of causing an increase in any allowance authorized under this title, or for the purpose of causing any allowance to be paid where none is authorized under this title, shall make or cause to be made any false statement or representation as to any wages paid or received, or whoever makes or causes to be made any false statement of a material fact in any claim for any allowance under this title, or whoever makes or causes to be made any false statement, representation, affidavit, or document in connection with such claim, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor and upon conviction thereof shall be fined not more than $1,000 or imprisoned for not more than 1 year, or both. 

Whoever shall obtain or receive any money, check, or allowance under this title, without being entitled thereto and with intent to defraud the United States, shall be punished by a fine of not more than $1,000 or by imprisonment for not more than 1 year, or both. 

SECTION 765. APPLICATION FOR READJUSTMENT ALLOWANCE 

The veteran's initial application for readjustment allowance, Form 

4-1382, Application for Servicemen's Readjustment Allowance-State 

Agency, may be filed through local offices or facilities of any public employment office or State unemployment agency designated to act for the Veterans' Administration. Applications may be made in person or by mail pursuant to local agency regulations and procedures. The agency may require any veteran who files an application and who has no social-security number to secure such a number.

The veteran at the time he files his application should present his discharge certificate or certificate evidencing the nature of his discharge or separation from military or naval service and the date and length of his active service. Original or properly authenticated copies of discharge or separation papers, or certificates in lieu of lost or destroyed discharges, shall be deemed sufficient evidence upon which to base a determination of entitlement and the number of weeks of allowances to which the veteran is entitled. A copy of a discharge or separation paper may be considered properly authenticated if such copy is certified by a notary public or other officer authorized by law to administer oaths or take acknowledgments. Photostats of original documents or of certified copies of records may be accepted if the original would be acceptable. 

Claim for readjustment allowance by self-employed veterans should be prepared on Form 4-1387, Veteráns Claim for Readjustment Allow ance and Statement of Net Earnings From Self-Employment for the Calendar Month. 

Claims of self-employed veterans should be filed not later than the last day of the calendar month following the calendar month for which the claim is filed, provided that if the claimant shows extenuating circumstances, the period for filing may be extended until not later than the tenth day of the second month following that for which the claim is filed.

If the veteran is unable to present sufficient evidence to support a determination of entitlement by the agency, the application will be cleared with the Veterans' Administration through the agent. Applications must be cleared with the Veterans' Administration: (1) Where veteran discharged after active service of less than 90 days by reason of injury or disability, unless the statement is made on the discharge or separation papers that he was discharged for disability incurred in service in line of duty. (2) Where veteran discharged or separated from service under other than honorable conditions. (3) Where question of active service of 90 days or more in doubt. 

4) Where period of active service cannot be determined due to periods of unauthorized absence for which pay was forfeited. (5) Where service was with an Allied Government.

Part F. Employment Assistance to Veterans SECTION 766. JOB COUNSELING AND EMPLOYMENT PLACEMENT SERVICE FOR VETERANS The Congress of the United States in title IV of the Servicemen's Readjustment Act of 1944, Public Law 346, Seventy-eighth Congress, 

June 22, 1944, as amended (38 U. S. C. 695–695f), declares as its intent and purpose that there shall be an effective job counseling and employment placement service for veterans and that to this end policies shall be promulgated and administered so as to provide for them the maximum of job opportunity in the field of gainful employment. 

In order to accomplish this purpose, the Congress created the 

Veterans' Placement Service Board composed of the Administrator of Veterans' Affairs as Chairman, the Director of the National Se lective Service System (now the Director of Selective Service), and the

official responsible for the functions of the United States Employment
